`privileged` run at dispatch: the adapter's compile decides ([ADR-0018](../adr/0018-claude-bypass-omits-restricted.md)
for Claude Code bypass runs).

```jsonc
{ "$defs": { "privileged_scope": { "type": "object", "additionalProperties": false,
    "required": ["files","shell","network","repository_config"], "properties": {
      "files": { "type": "object", "additionalProperties": false, "required": ["access"], "properties": {
          "access": { "enum": ["read-only","worktree"] } } },
      "shell": { "type": "object", "additionalProperties": false, "required": ["mode"], "properties": {
          "mode": { "enum": ["none","allowlist","broad"] },
          "allowlist": { "type": "array", "minItems": 1, "maxItems": 64, "items":       // required iff mode is allowlist
              { "type": "array", "minItems": 1, "maxItems": 64,                          // one argv-exact command
                "items": { "type": "string", "minLength": 1, "maxLength": 1024 } } } } },
      "network": { "type": "object", "additionalProperties": false, "required": ["mode"], "properties": {
          "mode": { "enum": ["off","allowlist","on"] },
          "hosts": { "type": "array", "minItems": 1, "maxItems": 64, "uniqueItems": true,  // required iff mode is allowlist
              "items": { "type": "string", "minLength": 1, "maxLength": 253 } } } },       // host names, no scheme or path
      "repository_config": { "type": "object", "additionalProperties": false, "required": ["mode"], "properties": {
          "mode": { "enum": ["disabled","approved","enabled"] } } },  // hooks, MCP definitions, agent instructions
      "bypass": { "type": "boolean", "default": false } } } } }      // provider bypass flag requested
```

```ts
// The structured type T3.3 narrows `PrivilegedScope` in src/policy/types.ts to (an open record today).
export interface PrivilegedScope {
  files: { access: 'read-only' | 'worktree' };
  shell: { mode: 'none' | 'broad' } | { mode: 'allowlist'; allowlist: string[][] };
  network: { mode: 'off' | 'on' } | { mode: 'allowlist'; hosts: string[] };
  repository_config: { mode: 'disabled' | 'approved' | 'enabled' };
  bypass: boolean;                                       // always present after defaults are filled
}
```

Meaning of each value, and how it lines up with the profile fields of `ResolvedProfile` (§2, `src/policy/profiles.ts`):

| Dimension | Values | Meaning | `ResolvedProfile` field |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `files.access` | `read-only`, `worktree` | Read only, or edits confined to the run's worktree. There is no wider value: a `privileged` run still works in a worktree (FR-WS-01) | `fileAccess` (same values) |
| `shell.mode` | `none`, `allowlist`, `broad` | No shell; only the listed commands; any command | `shell`, `shellAllowlist` (same values) |
| `shell.allowlist` | argv arrays | Each entry is one exact argv (program and arguments); no wildcard, no shell string | `shellAllowlist` |
| `network.mode` | `off`, `allowlist`, `on` | No network; only the listed hosts; unrestricted | `network`, `networkAllowlist`; `on` has no profile counterpart (the profile value `policy` is not offered in a scope, because it names no behaviour by itself) |
| `repository_config.mode` | `disabled`, `approved`, `enabled` | Repository-provided hooks, MCP definitions and agent instructions not loaded; only those the operator's configuration approves; all loaded | `repoConfig` `disabled`, `approved-only`, and a scope-only `enabled` (wider than `allowlisted`) |
| `bypass` | boolean, default `false` | The provider's bypass flag is requested; emitted only under the conditions in **Bypass** below | `bypassApproved` (set from the approval, never from the request) |

*Note (2026-10-13, owner decision 26, Q-37, [ADR-0021](../adr/0021-repository-config-in-approved-bypass-runs.md);
T3.19):* `approved` and `enabled` are compilable only in an approved bypass run (`bypass: true` and the run's
`bypassApproved`). The Claude adapter then omits `--safe-mode`, keeps `--strict-mcp-config` for `approved` and omits
it for `enabled`; the Git deny rules and the worktree are unchanged. Without that approval, and for a Codex scope (its
bypass is undocumented), they remain `Ineligible: CAPABILITY_MISSING`. Flag behaviour unverified (R-41).

**Validation** ([FRD §7.1](../requirements/FRD.md#71-delegate_task), in table order): a malformed scope fails schema
validation (V1, `INVALID_REQUEST`, `details.field` naming the offending path): every dimension is required, unknown keys
are refused at every level, `shell.allowlist` is present exactly when `shell.mode` is `allowlist` and `network.hosts`
exactly when `network.mode` is `allowlist` (the JSON Schema above cannot say "iff", so T3.3 checks it in code as part
of V1). `privileged` above the client's ceiling is `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` (V5, BR-13) whether or not a scope is present;
then V13: `privileged` without a scope, or a scope with any other profile, is `INVALID_REQUEST`
(`details.field = "privileged_scope"`). Entries are stored and shown verbatim, so they must not contain secrets; they are
never written to MCP envelopes or logs.

**Compilation.** The adapter compiles a `privileged` run from the scope in its policy snapshot, not from a configured
profile: `privileged` has no `profiles.<name>` override (T3.2). Each dimension maps to the provider's native control
(T3.5, T3.6). A dimension the provider cannot express makes the provider `Ineligible` for the run with a reason naming
the dimension (FRD BR-3); it is never dropped, widened or narrowed silently. The FR-GIT-01 Git write deny rules apply to
every compiled `privileged` invocation whatever the scope says; no scope value permits a Git write by the agent. If a
bypass flag would override those rules on a provider, that is R-26, not something a scope can grant.

**Canonical form and hash.** At admission (FRD T2), with defaults filled (`bypass` present, `false` when omitted):

```text
scope_json = canonicalJson(privileged_scope)                       // sorted keys, as request_hash (§10);
                                                                   // list order kept as given
scope_hash = hex(SHA-256(canonicalJson({ "v": 2,
                                         "client_id": run.client_id,
                                         "meta_session_id": run.meta_session_id,
                                         "scope": privileged_scope })))
```

`v: 1` (the hash over the run ID and `request_hash`) was never implemented and is withdrawn. The `v: 2` hash binds the
approval to one MCP client, one meta-session and one canonical scope (owner decision 17), not to one run, so it is the
same for every run of that meta-session that uses that scope, and different for any other client, meta-session or
scope. The run row, the `PENDING` approval row (`run_id` = this first run, `scope_json`, `scope_hash`, `requested_at`)
and the first `run_event` are written in one transaction; the policy snapshot records `privilegedScope` with the same
`scopeHash` and the approval's `approvalId`. An idempotent replay (§10) returns the existing run and creates no second
approval row. No schema change is needed: `approval.run_id` names the run that requested the approval, and the
meta-session and client are read from that run.

**Lookup.** A meta-session has at most one approval row in M3, because only `delegate_task` creates one and it always
starts a new meta-session. The approval of a meta-session is found by joining `approval.run_id` to `run` with the
meta-session ID and client ID (Proposed: T3.3 may add an index in a forward migration if the query needs one).

- `delegate_task` with `privileged` always creates a new meta-session, so it always creates a new `PENDING` row and an
  AWAITING_APPROVAL run, even when an identical scope was approved for another meta-session.
- `continue_task` whose resolved profile is `privileged` (requested, or defaulted from the previous run, FRD C4) looks
  up the meta-session's approval for the calling client. If it is `APPROVED`, the continuation takes the approved
  scope as is: `continue_task` has no scope input, so it cannot widen, narrow or otherwise change the scope (FRD C7).
  The new run is admitted to QUEUED (FRD T1) with `privilegedScope` copied from the approval (`scope`, `scopeHash`,
  `approvalId`) and a `run_event` naming the approval; no new row is written. If the row is `PENDING`, `DENIED` or
  absent, or the caller is a different client, the request is `INVALID_REQUEST` (C7) and nothing is written.
- A different scope means a new `delegate_task`, and therefore a new meta-session and a new `PENDING` approval; a
  different client never matches.

**Approve.** The operator reviews the scope and hash with `runs show`, then runs `approve <run_id>` (operator CLI
only, FR-OPS-02) on the run that requested the approval. It recomputes the hash from that run's `client_id`,
`meta_session_id` and the stored `scope_json`, and writes `APPROVED`, `decided_at` and `decided_by` only if the run is
AWAITING_APPROVAL, the row is `PENDING` and the recomputed hash equals `scope_hash`; otherwise it is a CLI error and
nothing changes. `approve` on a later run of the meta-session is a CLI error: it is not AWAITING_APPROVAL and has no
row of its own. `deny` writes `DENIED` and the run ends CANCELLED (WF-7). No MCP tool, timer or client ceiling
approves, except `decide_approval` for clients the operator marks `approval_proxy`
([§3.4.8](#348-approval-proxy-tools-fr-ctl-08-adr-0047), ADR-0047, Proposed), and no configuration value does except
the per-client `privileged_approval` setting below. An approval never
expires and is never revoked automatically (owner decision 17); a manual revoke is ROADMAP Q-30.

**Client policy (2026-10-13, owner decision 22; Delegated realisation, [ADR-0019](../adr/0019-per-client-privileged-approval.md)).**
Each `mcp_clients[]` entry has `privileged_approval`: `"required"` (default) or `"not_required"`, the latter valid only
with a `privileged` ceiling ([TDD §3.1](TDD.md#31-configuration)). For a `"required"` client everything above holds
unchanged. For a `"not_required"` client a `privileged` `delegate_task` passes the same validation (V1, V5, V13), the
same canonical form and the same `v: 2` hash, and is then admitted by FRD T1 to QUEUED instead of T2. In the admission
transaction the controller writes the approval row as for T2 and decides it at once: `status = 'APPROVED'`,
`decided_at` = admission time, `decided_by = "client_policy:<client_id>"`; the first `run_event` is the T1
`state_transition` naming the approval (`approval_id`, `approval: "client_policy"`), followed by an `approval_decided`
event (`approval_id`, `status`, `reason: "client_policy"`). No other check is relaxed: **Lookup**, C7 continuations and
**Bypass** treat the row like an operator approval; the FR-GIT-01 deny rules and the run's worktree (FR-WS-05) apply.
`approve` and `deny` refuse such a run (it is never AWAITING_APPROVAL) and name the client policy. The envelope shows
`approval.status` `APPROVED` and never `decided_by`.

**Bypass.** Before compiling any `privileged` run, COMP-POL loads the approval named by the snapshot's `approvalId` and
sets `bypassApproved = true` only if: the row is `APPROVED`; its `scope_hash` equals the snapshot's `scopeHash` and the
hash recomputed from this run's `client_id`, `meta_session_id` and snapshot scope; and the approved scope has
`bypass: true`. Any mismatch fails the run `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` before spawn, with a `run_event` naming the mismatch
(no process, no flag). With `bypassApproved` false the adapter compiles `privileged` without a bypass flag. The argv
audit (T3.17) independently refuses any `BYPASS_FLAGS` entry (and every declared form of §2, ADR-0035, ADR-0037)
unless the profile is `privileged`, `bypassApproved` is true and the snapshot's scope sets `bypass` (the lifecycle
passes the run's resolved profile and `bypassApproved` to `startAdapterRun`, so the audit sees the same values the
compile did). The compiled `policyFlags` are recorded in the
run's events, so every bypass run's invocation and the approval it ran under are in the audit trail.

**Listing (Proposed, Delegated, 2026-10-13).** `list_capabilities` lists `privileged` in a provider's
`supported_profiles` when its adapter compiles **any** scope of a small representative set, all with `bypass: false`
(listing never compiles with `bypassApproved`): (1) the narrowest scope (`read-only` files, shell `none`, network `off`,
repository configuration `disabled`); (2) `worktree` files, shell `none`, network `on`, repository configuration
`disabled`; (3) `worktree` files, shell `broad`, network `off`, repository configuration `disabled`. No single scope
compiles for both M3 adapters (Claude Code has no network control, so it compiles network `on` only; Codex cannot
disable commands, so it needs shell `broad` with network `off`), so the narrowest scope alone would list `privileged` for
nobody. A listed `privileged` means "this provider compiles at least one privileged scope here"; each request's own
scope is still compiled at dispatch, where an `Ineligible` ends the run with its BR-3 code. Code:
`REPRESENTATIVE_PRIVILEGED_SCOPES` in `src/mcp/server.ts`.

**What is shown where.** The envelope (`get_run`, `delegate_task`, `continue_task`, `cancel_run`) carries only
`approval.status` and `approval.requested_at` for a `privileged` run; it never contains the scope content, the hash or
`decided_by` (FR-MCP-04). `runs show` (operator CLI) shows the full scope, the `bypass` value, the scope hash, the
approval decision and, for an approval, every run of the meta-session that ran under it.

**Implementation notes (T3.3, Proposed, Delegated).** Code: `src/policy/scope.ts` (schema, list checks, canonical
form, hash, spelling maps), `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts` (admission), `src/ledger/repo.ts` (approval helpers).

- Spellings: neither enum is renamed. A `privileged` snapshot keeps `scoped` in the four scalar fields and the scope in
  its own spellings; `src/policy/scope.ts` maps them (`on` and `enabled` have no profile value; `approved` is
  `approved-only`) for the adapters (T3.5, T3.6).
- `network.hosts` entries must match a bare host-name pattern (no scheme, port, path or wildcard).
- V11 for `privileged` defers the adapter's compile verdict to dispatch ([FRD V11](../requirements/FRD.md#71-delegate_task)),
  because `bypassApproved` is known only there. *Changed 2026-10-13 (T3.18, owner decisions 19 and 22):* the earlier
  blanket dispatch refusal of every `privileged` run ("privileged compile not available until T3.5/T3.6") is removed.
  After the **Bypass** check passes, the lifecycle appends a `privileged_dispatch` event (`approval_id`,
  `bypass_approved`) and hands the snapshot with that `bypassApproved` to the preflight eligibility compile and the
  LEASED compile; an adapter `Ineligible` ends the run with its BR-3 code (FRD T8 or T10), as for any profile. A stored
  `bypassApproved` is never trusted: the lifecycle resets it to `false` for every run before the check.
- Cancelling a run in AWAITING_APPROVAL closes its `PENDING` row as `DENIED` in the same transaction (ROADMAP Q-15
  default): `decided_by` is `cancel_run` for the MCP tool and the requesting OS user for `runs cancel`, plus an
  `approval_closed` `run_event` (approval ID and status only).
- The §1 guard lets any column change while a row is `PENDING`. The repo helper updates only `status`, `decided_at` and
  `decided_by`; the hash re-check (C7 here, `approve` in T3.4) catches a scope rewritten without its hash, but not a
  writer with ledger access who rewrites both.
- No index was added: the meta-session lookup joins `approval.run_id` to `run` and expects small tables.

**Implementation notes (T3.4, Proposed, Delegated).** Code: `src/lifecycle/approval.ts` (decision rules, application,
bypass verdict), `src/cli/operator.ts` and `src/cli/runs.ts` (`approve`, `deny`), `src/reconcile/reconcile.ts`.

- `approve` and `deny` follow the `runs cancel` pattern ([ADR-0005](../adr/0005-sqlite-ledger.md), [TDD
  §3.5](TDD.md#35-concurrency-model)): after the checks in **Approve** above, the CLI appends an
  `operator_decision_requested` `run_event` (decision, approval ID, OS user, the scope hash it checked) in the same
  transaction. With no daemon (the CLI holds the data-directory lock) it applies the decision at once; otherwise `serve`
  applies it on its operator poll, or startup reconciliation does. Applying re-runs every check, then writes the row
  (`decided_at` is the time of the CLI's record, `decided_by` its OS user), an `approval_decided` event, the run's
  transition (T3 to QUEUED, or T19 to CANCELLED) and an `operator_decision_applied` event in one transaction, so the row
  and the run state change together; a decision that no longer applies gets `operator_decision_rejected` and changes
  nothing. While a decision or a cancel is pending for the run, a further `approve` or `deny` is refused.
- The **Approve** checks also require that the run's snapshot names this approval and carries the same `scopeHash`, and
  that the hash recomputed from the row's stored `scope_json` equals `scope_hash`; the **Bypass** check applies the same
  three hash comparisons and also requires the row to have been requested by a run of the same meta-session and client.
- The **Bypass** check runs in `RunLifecycle` at dispatch (PREFLIGHT, before any probe), not in `src/policy`; a mismatch
  is FRD T8 with `PERMISSION_BLOCKED`, and the transition event names the failed check (never a hash or scope content).
- `get_run include_events` drops `requested_by` and `scope_hash` from `operator_decision_requested` events.
- An approved run is QUEUED without the ingress queue bound (FRD T3 has no such guard).

### 3.2 Provider and model affinity (M7)

Implements FR-RTE-06..09, FR-CAP-08 and [ADR-0032](../adr/0032-provider-model-affinity.md) (owner decision 40).
Everything here is **Proposed**; thresholds are Assumptions with no value fixed, chosen in M6 and M7 and recorded then.
The mechanism extends the two-stage routing of [ADR-0008](../adr/0008-two-stage-routing.md): the hard filter (FR-RTE-01)
is unchanged and runs first, and `require_provider` and the hints of FR-MCP-05 narrow it as before.

*T7.0 (2026-10-14, Delegated):* [§17](#17-routing-m7) fills the points this section leaves open: the defaults (both
thresholds `null`, so the estimate trigger is off), when the row is written, how each reason is detected, continuations,
the reset record and three added columns ([§17.6](#176-affinity-details-fills-32)).

**Configuration.** Part of the `routing` object of the config schema (TDD §3.1):

```jsonc
{ "routing": { "affinity": {
    "enabled": true,            // default true; false = M7 scoring behaviour unchanged, no affinity row is read or written
    "low_water_mark": "<chosen in M6/M7>",   // FR-CAP-02 estimate at or below which the affine provider counts as maxed out
    "min_confidence": "<chosen in M6/M7>",   // lowest estimate confidence at which the low-water mark counts (ADR-0007)
    "idle_ttl": null            // default none: affinity never expires by time (Q-52)
} } }
```

An unknown key or type error aborts startup (NFR-SEC-06). `low_water_mark` and `min_confidence` have no default; while
either is unset, the estimate-based trigger is off and only `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED` (FR-CAP-06) triggers `capacity_exhausted`.

**Table** (forward migration at M7, ADR-0005; mutable current state, history is the append-only `route_decision` rows
and the `routing_affinity_*` run events):

```sql
CREATE TABLE project_affinity (         -- one row per project key (FR-RTE-06)
  project_key        TEXT PRIMARY KEY,  -- project id, or 'ws:' || SHA-256 of the canonical workspace_path (no project)
  provider           TEXT NOT NULL,
  model              TEXT,              -- last model the provider ran for this project; NULL = adapter default (FR-RTE-08)
  since              TEXT NOT NULL,     -- when this provider became the affinity
  last_run_id        TEXT REFERENCES run(id),   -- last run that completed on it
  last_switch_reason TEXT CHECK (last_switch_reason IS NULL OR last_switch_reason IN
                       ('user_request','capacity_exhausted','ineligible','failed_after_retry')),
  version            INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0
) STRICT;
```

A per-provider model memory is kept in the same row's `model` and in `route_decision`; the controller reuses the last
model for the chosen provider only (FR-RTE-08).

**Algorithm** (runs inside the routing step of dispatch, one ledger transaction with the route decision):

1. Resolve the project key. Read `project_affinity` (none if the row is missing or `enabled` is false).
2. Run the hard filter (FR-RTE-01) to get candidates with reasons.
3. Determine the **switch reason**, first match wins, in this order:
   1. `user_request`: `require_provider`, an explicit provider or model in the request, or an operator `affinity reset`
      since the last run. A `provider_preference` that only narrows or orders candidates is a hint, not a request for a
      different provider, and is not a reason (FR-MCP-05).
   2. `ineligible`: the affine provider is absent from the candidates for a reason other than capacity (capability,
      profile, auth, version, manifest, circuit breaker open).
   3. `capacity_exhausted`: the affine provider was removed for known quota exhaustion, a `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED` run just
      failed over (FR-CAP-06), or the FR-CAP-02 estimate is at or below `low_water_mark` with confidence at least
      `min_confidence`.
   4. `failed_after_retry`: FR-CAP-05 retries on the affine provider are exhausted for this meta-session.
4. If there is an affinity and **no** reason applies: select the affine provider, and (FR-RTE-08) the project's last
   model for it unless the request names one. Scores are not used; the decision stores the candidate list and reasons as
   usual.
5. Otherwise (no affinity, or a reason applies): rank the remaining eligible candidates with the FR-RTE-02 score, select
   the best, and write the new `project_affinity` row (`since` = now, `last_switch_reason` = the reason, or NULL for a
   first run). A model changes only for `user_request` or `ineligible` (FR-RTE-08); otherwise the new provider uses its
   adapter default or the project's remembered model for that provider.
6. If the affinity was lost and no candidate remains, the outcome is `ELIGIBILITY_NONE` as today, with the reason
   recorded; the affinity row is left unchanged.
7. When a run reaches `SUCCEEDED`, set `last_run_id` and `model` (as reported by the adapter) on the row; a first run
   that fails does not create affinity. The controller never moves affinity on recovery of the old provider (Q-51).

`affinity reset` is an operator command that records an operator row (like `approve`); the daemon applies it at the next
routing step as a `user_request` for that project. Scores, cost and load are never a reason.

**Routing-decision fields (FR-RTE-03).** `route_decision.reasons_json` gains an `affinity` object (no new column):

```jsonc
{ "affinity": {
    "enabled": true,
    "project_key": "proj_x",
    "provider": "<affine provider, or null>",
    "model": "<affine model, or null>",
    "held": true,                              // the affine provider and model were chosen
    "affinity_switch_reason": null,            // 'user_request' | 'capacity_exhausted' | 'ineligible' | 'failed_after_retry' | null
    "scored": false                            // the FR-RTE-02 score ranked candidates in this decision
} }
```

Invariants checked by the replay test (TDD §3.13.3): `held` implies `affinity_switch_reason` is null and `scored` is
false; a changed provider or model implies a non-null reason; `scored` is true only when there was no affinity or a
reason applied. `explain_route` returns this object. The envelope's `route.reasons` list names the reason code when
affinity ended.

**Prompt assembly order (FR-RTE-09, Could).** The delegated prompt is assembled in a fixed order: stable content first
(project instructions, constraints, trust and tool preamble), then volatile content (task text, retrieved snippets of
§14.6) last. The order of stable parts is fixed per project and not shuffled between runs. This is a hygiene rule; no
provider is claimed to reuse a prefix cache, and the benefit is unverified (Q-53).

**Cache and switch accounting (FR-CAP-08, M6).** Where an adapter's parsed output reports cache read or creation token
counts, they are stored in `usage_observation.metrics_json` (shape owned by the adapter contract, §2) labelled with the
existing `source`; an absent count stays absent and is never estimated. The per-project figures (runs that kept the
previous provider and model, runs that switched, by reason) are a query over `route_decision.reasons_json`, exposed by
`get_capacity` as an optional `affinity` block and by `doctor`; they are evidence for the operator, not a claim of hits.

**Events** (`run_event.type`, M7, bounded payloads): `routing_affinity_held` (`project_key`, `provider`, `model`),
`routing_affinity_switched` (`from_provider`, `to_provider`, `from_model`, `to_model`, `reason`),
`routing_affinity_reset` (`project_key`, `by`). None is a failure code or changes a run's status.

### 3.3 Network MCP transport (M13)

Design gate: [ADR-0041](../adr/0041-http-transport-with-oauth-2-1.md) (Accepted 2026-10-14 under T13.1).
Requirements FR-MCP-12 to 17. Off unless the config enables it.

- **Endpoint.** One Streamable HTTP path (`/mcp`), bound to `127.0.0.1` unless `transport.http.bind` is set explicitly.
  Every request checks the `Origin` header against `transport.http.allowed_origins` before routing; an `Origin` that is
  not listed (including `null`) is refused with HTTP 403. A request with no `Origin` header is not a browser
  cross-origin request and passes this check (T13.2, Delegated: browsers send `Origin` on every cross-origin request
  and on every `POST`, which is what DNS rebinding needs); the token check still applies to it. No CORS headers are
  sent yet.
- **Config (T13.1, as built; key names other than `bind` Delegated).** `transport.http`: `enabled` (default false),
  `public_url` (the `https://` MCP endpoint URL behind the operator's TLS terminator; no userinfo, query or fragment;
  a path that is not `/`, under `/.well-known/` or an authorization-server endpoint), `bind` (an IP literal, default
  `127.0.0.1`), `port`, `allow_non_loopback` (default false), `allowed_origins` (T13.2: at most 32 serialised origins
  such as `https://app.example`, exactly as a browser sends them; default empty, which refuses every request carrying
  an `Origin`). `public_url` and `port` are required when enabled. Plain
  HTTP in `public_url` is refused at load, even for localhost. Code: `src/config/http-transport.ts`.
- **Metadata.** `GET /.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` and the path-suffixed form
  `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/<endpoint path>` (RFC 9728 §3.1) return `resource` (the normalised
  `public_url`), `authorization_servers` (the embedded issuer: the origin of `public_url`), `bearer_methods_supported:
  ["header"]` and `resource_name`. `GET /.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` (RFC 8414) returns the issuer, the
  `/authorize`, `/token` and `/register` endpoints, `response_types_supported: ["code"]`, `grant_types_supported:
  ["authorization_code", "refresh_token"]`, `code_challenge_methods_supported: ["S256"]` and
  `token_endpoint_auth_methods_supported: ["none"]`. Each of those three endpoints answers 404 when `serve` has not
  configured it (registration: T13.7; authorization and token: T13.8). Code: `src/mcp/http.ts`.
- **Challenge.** A request without a valid bearer token gets HTTP 401 with `WWW-Authenticate` naming the metadata URL.
  The server never returns a token or any authorization detail.
- **Verification.** Signature or introspection, `exp`, `iss` equal to the configured issuer, `aud` equal to the resource
  identifier (RFC 8707). Failure is 401; no fallback.
- **Tokens as built (T13.3, Q-73, Delegated).** Access and refresh tokens are opaque, never JWTs: 32 bytes from the
  CSPRNG, base64url, behind the prefix `sbat_` (access) or `sbrt_` (refresh). Only the SHA-256 hash is stored, in
  `oauth_token` (migration 0010, DDL below); verification is a lookup by hash with a constant-time compare of the stored
  hash, which is the local introspection FR-MCP-13 allows. A token this server did not issue (forged, or from another
  issuer) has no row, so there is no signature or `iss` to check. Refused alike: no single well-formed `Bearer`
  credential (`malformed`), no row (`unknown`), a refresh token presented as a bearer token (`wrong_kind`), `revoked`,
  `expired` (from the expiry instant on) and a `resource` other than this server's (`wrong_audience`). Each gets the same
  401 `{"error":"invalid_token"}` with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer error="invalid_token", resource_metadata="<metadata
  URL>"`; a request with no `Authorization` header gets the challenge without an error code (RFC 6750 §3.1). A token in
  the query string is not read. The reason code is logged (`refused an HTTP bearer token`, `reason`) and nothing else.
  A valid token goes on to the mapping below (T13.4). Code:
  `src/mcp/oauth-tokens.ts`, `src/mcp/http.ts`.

```sql
CREATE TABLE oauth_token (               -- migration 0010 (T13.3); mutable only in revoked/revoked_at
  token_hash  TEXT PRIMARY KEY CHECK (length(token_hash) = 64 AND token_hash NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*'),  -- sha256 hex
  kind        TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (kind IN ('access','refresh')),
  client_id   TEXT NOT NULL,
  resource    TEXT NOT NULL,             -- audience (RFC 8707)
  scopes_json TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (json_valid(scopes_json) AND json_type(scopes_json) = 'array'),
  issued_at   TEXT NOT NULL,
  expires_at  TEXT NOT NULL,
  revoked     INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (revoked IN (0,1)),
  revoked_at  TEXT
) STRICT;
CREATE INDEX oauth_token_client ON oauth_token(client_id, kind);
```

- **Client registration as built (T13.7, Q-74, Delegated).** `POST /register` (RFC 7591) needs no initial access
  token, because a remote client registers itself, but a registered client is `pending` and gets no code or token until
  the operator approves it on the consent page (a later task). The body must be `application/json`, at most 16 KiB
  (413 otherwise, 415 for another type). Accepted: `redirect_uris` (1 to 4 absolute `https:` URLs of at most 2048
  characters, no userinfo or fragment, no duplicates; plain `http:`, loopback included, is refused),
  `token_endpoint_auth_method` absent or `none`, `grant_types` absent (default `authorization_code`) or a subset of
  `authorization_code` and `refresh_token` containing `authorization_code`, `response_types` absent or `["code"]`, and
  an optional `client_name` of 1 to 128 printable characters. Other fields (`scope`, `jwks`, `software_statement`, ...)
  are ignored and not echoed. Errors are RFC 7591's `invalid_redirect_uri` or `invalid_client_metadata` with a
  description that echoes no input. The 201 response carries `client_id` (`sbc_` and 128 random bits),
  `client_id_issued_at`, `client_name`, `redirect_uris`, `grant_types`, `response_types: ["code"]` and
  `token_endpoint_auth_method: "none"`; no secret and no registration access token (RFC 7592 is not offered). At most 16
  `pending` clients created in the last 24 hours may exist; another registration gets 429 `temporarily_unavailable`
  with `Retry-After: 3600`. The endpoint passes the `Origin` check first. `serve` logs the new `client_id` only. Code:
  `src/mcp/oauth-clients.ts`, `src/mcp/http.ts`.

```sql
CREATE TABLE oauth_client (              -- migration 0011 (T13.7); status changes only by operator decision
  client_id          TEXT PRIMARY KEY CHECK (client_id GLOB 'sbc_*'),
  client_name        TEXT,
  redirect_uris_json TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (json_valid(redirect_uris_json) AND json_type(redirect_uris_json) = 'array'),
  grant_types_json   TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (json_valid(grant_types_json) AND json_type(grant_types_json) = 'array'),
  status             TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending' CHECK (status IN ('pending','approved','denied','revoked')),
  created_at         TEXT NOT NULL,
  decided_at         TEXT
) STRICT;
CREATE INDEX oauth_client_status ON oauth_client(status, created_at);
```

- **Authorization, consent and token endpoints as built (T13.8, Q-75, Q-76, Delegated).** `GET /authorize` requires
  `response_type=code`, a registered `client_id`, a `redirect_uri` equal to a registered one,
  `code_challenge_method=S256` with a 43-character `code_challenge`, and `resource` equal to the resource identifier;
  `scope` is absent or `mcp` (the only grantable scope; `privileged` and anything else get `invalid_scope`); `state` is
  at most 512 characters; no parameter may repeat. An unknown client or unregistered redirect URI gets an HTML error
  page and no redirect; every other error redirects with the RFC 6749 error code, `state` and `iss` (RFC 9207) for an
  approved client only, and is an error page for a pending, denied or revoked one (no open redirector through a
  self-registered URI, RFC 9700 §4.11.2). A valid request shows the consent page: static HTML (escaped client name,
  client id, redirect URI, scope), with `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; form-action 'self' <redirect
  origin>; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'none'`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Cache-Control: no-store`,
  `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`. The consent request (random id, CSRF token hash, PKCE challenge, at most 32 open; when
  full, the oldest request of a not-yet-approved client is dropped first; 10 minutes) is held in memory; the CSRF token
  is in the form and in a `__Host-sb_consent` cookie (`Secure; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict`). `POST /authorize` (form, at
  most 8 KiB) needs both CSRF copies, an `Origin` absent or equal to the issuer (the one origin the global `Origin`
  check lets through on this path) and `Sec-Fetch-Site` absent or `same-origin`; a CSRF failure ends the request. "Deny"
  redirects with `access_denied` and changes no client. "Approve" needs `transport.http.passphrase_hash` to verify the
  passphrase (scrypt N=2^17, r=8, p=1, 16-byte salt, 32-byte key, `timingSafeEqual`; one check at a time) or a pairing
  code to redeem; five wrong passphrases in 15 minutes lock passphrase approval for 15 minutes (429, `Retry-After`),
  during which the right passphrase is refused. Wrong pairing codes are throttled separately (ten a minute, then 429
  without consuming the code) and never locked, so a stranger who keeps the passphrase locked cannot also block the
  operator's fresh pairing code. Success approves a `pending` client and redirects (303) with a single-use code (`sbac_`
  and 256 bits, kept hashed in memory for 60 seconds). `POST /token` (form; `client_id` required, no client
  authentication) accepts `authorization_code` (`code`, `redirect_uri`, `code_verifier`; the code is spent by any
  attempt; `S256(code_verifier)` must equal the challenge; a reused code revokes the client's tokens) and
  `refresh_token` (only for a client registered with that grant; the client, status and scope checks run before the
  token is spent, then `OAuthTokenStore.useRefreshToken` revokes it in one transaction, so of two concurrent rotations
  one fails; a reused token revokes all the client's tokens; `scope` may only narrow). `resource`, if sent, must equal
  the resource identifier; every token is bound to it. Access tokens last 15 minutes, refresh tokens 7 days. Responses
  carry `Cache-Control: no-store`; errors are `{"error": ...}` only. `serve` logs `OAuth authorization event` with the
  event name, client id, method and reason code, never a value. Code: `src/mcp/oauth-server.ts`,
  `src/mcp/oauth-consent-page.ts`, `src/mcp/oauth-passphrase.ts`.
- **Pairing codes (T13.8, Q-76).** `switchboard consent pair` creates 12 Crockford base32 characters (60 bits, shown
  `XXXX-XXXX-XXXX`), valid once for 10 minutes; creating one ends every older unused one. Only the SHA-256 of the
  normalised code is stored; redemption is one `UPDATE ... WHERE used_at IS NULL AND expires_at > now`. `consent
  hash-passphrase` reads only a pipe, never a terminal (no echo into scrollback). Code: `src/mcp/oauth-pairing.ts`,
  `src/cli/consent.ts`.

```sql
CREATE TABLE oauth_pairing_code (        -- migration 0012 (T13.8); mutable only in used_at
  code_hash  TEXT PRIMARY KEY CHECK (length(code_hash) = 64 AND code_hash NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*'),  -- sha256 hex
  created_at TEXT NOT NULL,
  expires_at TEXT NOT NULL,
  used_at    TEXT                        -- redeemed or superseded
) STRICT;
```

- **Mapping.** `subject` (and an optional client claim) maps to a configured client entry (`transport.http.clients`).
  The entry's `max_profile` is frozen into the policy snapshot at admission (FR-MCP-06, FR-POL-01). No claim can name
  `privileged`.
- **Mapping and mount as built (T13.4, Q-77, Delegated).** The token's subject is its OAuth client id.
  `transport.http.clients` lists `{ "oauth_client_id": "sbc_...", "mcp_client": "<mcp_clients id>" }` (at most 64, each
  OAuth client once); `transport.http.default_mcp_client` optionally names the entry for an approved OAuth client with
  no mapping. Config load refuses a target that is not in `mcp_clients` or that has `privileged_approval: not_required`,
  so a remote client's `privileged` runs always wait for the operator CLI (FR-MCP-14, ADR-0019), and a
  `default_mcp_client` with a `privileged` ceiling (only an explicit mapping may reach one). The token's OAuth client
  must still be `approved` on every request (401 otherwise, also when no client store is wired). At the MCP endpoint a
  valid token without the `mcp` scope, or whose client has no mapping, gets 403 `{"error":"insufficient_scope"}` with
  `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer error="insufficient_scope", scope="mcp", resource_metadata=...`; `serve` logs the reason and
  the OAuth client id. Only `POST` is served (405 with `Allow: POST` otherwise: a stateless `GET` would hold an endless
  event stream). At most 16 requests are in flight, 4 per OAuth client (503 with `Retry-After: 1`), and a body is at
  most 256 KiB (Assumptions). The `Authorization` and `Cookie` headers are removed before the SDK copies headers into
  the handlers' request info. Otherwise the request is served in the MCP SDK's stateless Streamable HTTP mode: a fresh
  `createSwitchboardServer` for the mapped client (the same lifecycle, ledger, projects and policy as stdio, so the same
  ceiling and admission checks) and a fresh transport per request, JSON responses, closed when the response ends. Token
  scopes and claims never reach the server, so nothing in a token can raise the profile. Runs are recorded under the MCP
  client id; T13.5 also binds them to the OAuth client (below). Code: `src/mcp/http.ts`,
  `src/config/http-transport.ts`, `src/config/load.ts`, `src/cli/serve.ts`.
- **Sessions.** The session identifier is random and stored with the subject and client; a mismatch returns HTTP 403 and
  an audit event with no token material. *As built (T13.4, Q-77):* the transport is stateless, so no session identifier
  is issued or accepted; every request authenticates with its own token.
- **Session binding as built (T13.5, Q-78, Delegated).** A request with an `Mcp-Session-Id` header is refused with 400
  `{"error":"invalid_request"}` before a server is built. What a caller returns to is a meta-session and its runs, so
  those are bound: the server for an HTTP request carries the token's OAuth client id as its subject, and every
  meta-session that `delegate_task` opens is recorded in `http_subject` (migration 0013, DDL below) right after
  admission. A meta-session, and every run in it (automatic retry successors included), is then known only to its own
  configured MCP client and its own subject: over HTTP the row must name the caller's OAuth client, over stdio there
  must be no row. `get_run`, `cancel_run`, `get_artifacts` and `continue_task` from another subject, from another
  client or from stdio get exactly the `INVALID_REQUEST` "unknown run" or "unknown meta-session" answer of an id that
  does not exist, with nothing changed (not the 403 above: a different status would be an existence oracle). An
  idempotency key another subject used gets the same answer as a key reused with different parameters. A binding never
  changes hands, and an HTTP server built without the binding store refuses to start. Dynamic projects (T9.9) stay per
  MCP client. Code: `src/mcp/http-subjects.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `src/mcp/http.ts`, `src/cli/serve.ts`.

```sql
CREATE TABLE http_subject (              -- migration 0013 (T13.5); insert-only, no foreign key (retention GC)
  meta_session_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  oauth_client_id TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (oauth_client_id GLOB 'sbc_*'),
  created_at      TEXT NOT NULL
) STRICT;
```

- **Redaction at the telemetry boundary (T13.5, FR-MCP-15).** The secret redactor (`src/artifacts/redact.ts`) also
  matches Switchboard's own `sbat_`, `sbrt_` and `sbac_` values, so a token or code in a prompt is redacted at admission
  (Q-68) and one in a tool result or error is redacted by the MCP scrubber. Every `serve` log line passes the same
  redactor before it is written to stderr. Pairing codes and passphrases have no distinctive shape and are never passed
  to a log or a response at all.
- **Forbidden.** Forwarding the inbound token, logging it, or storing it in the ledger.

### 3.4 Control plane (M14, Proposed, T14.0)

Design gate for FR-CTL-01 to 11, [ADR-0047](../adr/0047-approval-proxy-over-mcp.md) and
[ADR-0048](../adr/0048-notification-sinks.md) (both Proposed). Owner decisions 49 to 55 (merged in #388). Everything
here is **Proposed** and **Delegated** unless an SRS ID or a Proposed ADR fixes it. **No new run state** is added: pause
holds admission only; `needs_attention` is derived on read (FR-CTL-03); the FRD state machine is unchanged.

**Components (SDD).** COMP-MCP exposes the tools and applies `tool_profile` at `tools/list`. COMP-LED supplies
`run`, `run_event`, `approval` and project revision facts. COMP-RTE enforces pause, budgets, the loop breaker and the
successor cap at admission. COMP-POL applies approval-proxy binding and records proxied decisions. COMP-TEL builds
closed sink payloads and delivers them off the run path ([HLD](HLD.md#3-trust-boundaries) TB-13, TB-14). COMP-EXE and
COMP-ADP implement `steer_run` at a boundary (T14.7).

#### 3.4.1 Shared types

Extend `$defs` in §3.0:

```jsonc
{ "$defs": {
  "needs_attention_reason": { "enum": [
      "awaiting_approval", "parked_loop", "failed_after_retry", "stalled", "budget_exhausted" ] },
  "needs_attention": { "type": "object", "required": ["active"], "properties": {
      "active": { "type": "boolean" },
      "reason": { "$ref": "#/$defs/needs_attention_reason" } } },  // required when active is true
  "digest_cursor": { "type": "string", "pattern": "^evt_[0-9A-HJKMNP-TV-Z]{26}$" },
  "untrusted_text": { "type": "object", "required": ["text","label"], "properties": {
      "text": { "type": "string", "maxLength": 500 },
      "label": { "const": "untrusted_agent_text" } } },
  "approval_kind": { "enum": ["privileged_scope","dynamic_project","push_revision"] },
  "approval_proxy_item": { "type": "object", "required": [
      "approval_id","approval_digest","kind","requested_at","target_client_id" ], "properties": {
      "approval_id": { "type": "string" },
      "approval_digest": { "type": "string", "pattern": "^[0-9a-f]{64}$" },
      "kind": { "$ref": "#/$defs/approval_kind" },
      "project_id": { "type": "string" },
      "run_id": { "type": "string" },
      "revision": { "type": "integer" },
      "target_client_id": { "type": "string" },
      "scope_summary": { "type": "string", "maxLength": 512 },   // controller-generated only
      "requested_at": { "type": "string" } } },
  "sink_event_kind": { "enum": [
      "run_terminal","needs_attention","approval_pending","approval_decided","budget_exhausted","project_paused" ] },
  "notification_payload": { "type": "object", "required": [
      "project_id","event_kind","reason_code","run_id","link" ], "properties": {
      "project_id": { "type": "string" },
      "event_kind": { "$ref": "#/$defs/sink_event_kind" },
      "reason_code": { "type": "string" },   // needs_attention reason or approval_kind
      "run_id": { "type": ["string","null"] },
      "link": { "type": "string", "maxLength": 2048 } } } } }
```

The compact **envelope** (§3.0) gains optional `needs_attention` (FR-CTL-03) on `get_run`, `list_runs` and digest run
rows. When `active` is false, `reason` is absent. Derivation rules are in §3.4.5; implementation is T14.2.

#### 3.4.2 `list_runs` (FR-CTL-01)

```jsonc
{ "input": { "type": "object", "properties": {
    "project_id": { "type": "string" },
    "status": { "$ref": "#/$defs/status" },
    "status_in": { "type": "array", "items": { "$ref": "#/$defs/status" }, "maxItems": 16 },
    "since": { "type": "string" },          // ISO-8601 lower bound on run.created_at, inclusive
    "until": { "type": "string" },          // ISO-8601 upper bound, exclusive
    "limit": { "type": "integer", "minimum": 1, "maximum": 200, "default": 50 },
    "cursor": { "type": "string", "maxLength": 128 } } },   // opaque pagination within the filter
  "output": { "type": "object", "required": ["runs"], "properties": {
    "runs": { "type": "array", "items": { "$ref": "#/$defs/envelope" } },
    "next_cursor": { "type": ["string","null"] } } } }
```

**Scoping.** Same rules as `get_run` (T14.1 corrected the earlier wording, Delegated): the caller sees only runs it
started (`client_id` match, and over HTTP only meta-sessions of its own subject, FR-MCP-15), so every row is the
envelope `get_run` returns for that run and no row exists that `get_run` would refuse. A run of another client is never
listed, even in a project both clients may use. The project rule narrows the filter, not the rows: `project_id` must
name a project the caller may use (`mcp_clients[].projects`, or a dynamic project it created and has not deleted). An
id that is neither configured nor one of the caller's dynamic projects (including another client's dynamic project) →
`INVALID_REQUEST`; a configured project outside the caller's list → `PERMISSION_BLOCKED`. Rows are ordered by
`created_at` descending, then `run_id` descending. Implementation: `src/mcp/server.ts` (`listRuns`),
`Ledger.listRunsForClient` (read-only); tests: `src/mcp/list-runs.test.ts`.

**Input details (T14.1, Proposed, Delegated).** The input object is closed. `status` and `status_in` are alternatives
(both → `INVALID_REQUEST`, `details.field = "status_in"`); `status_in` is non-empty and duplicate-free. `since` and
`until` are ISO-8601 date-times with seconds and a zone (`Z` or `±hh:mm`), compared as instants (normalised to UTC);
an impossible date is `INVALID_REQUEST`. `project_id` uses the configuration's project-id pattern. `next_cursor` is
always present: a string when more rows match, else `null`. The cursor is the last row's keyset position
(`created_at`, `run_id`) encoded as base64url; a cursor that does not decode to one is `INVALID_REQUEST`
(`details.field = "cursor"`). Scoping is re-applied on every page, so a cursor never widens what the caller sees.

#### 3.4.3 `get_digest` (FR-CTL-02)

```jsonc
{ "input": { "type": "object", "properties": {
    "since_cursor": { "$ref": "#/$defs/digest_cursor" } } },   // absent = full snapshot (within retention)
  "output": { "type": "object", "required": ["projects","cursor"], "properties": {
    "cursor": { "$ref": "#/$defs/digest_cursor" },
    "projects": { "type": "array", "items": { "type": "object", "required": ["project_id"], "properties": {
      "project_id": { "type": "string" },
      "paused": { "type": "boolean" },
      "budget": { "type": "object", "properties": {
          "exhausted": { "type": "boolean" },
          "caps": { "type": "array", "items": { "type": "object", "required": ["key","limit","used"], "properties": {
              "key": { "enum": ["runs_per_day","concurrent_runs","wall_clock_per_run","usage_per_window"] },
              "limit": { "type": ["integer","null"] },   // null = unset (never shown as a provider limit)
              "used": { "type": "integer" },
              "window_ends_at": { "type": ["string","null"] } } } } } },
      "needs_attention": { "type": "array", "items": { "type": "object", "required": [
          "run_id","reason" ], "properties": {
          "run_id": { "type": "string" },
          "reason": { "$ref": "#/$defs/needs_attention_reason" },
          "envelope": { "$ref": "#/$defs/envelope" } } } },
      "finished": { "type": "array", "items": { "$ref": "#/$defs/envelope" } },
      "failed": { "type": "array", "items": { "$ref": "#/$defs/envelope" } },
      "pending_approvals": { "type": "array", "items": { "$ref": "#/$defs/approval_proxy_item" } },
      "untrusted_summaries": { "type": "array", "items": { "type": "object", "required": [
          "run_id","field" ], "properties": {
          "run_id": { "type": "string" },
          "field": { "enum": ["result.summary"] },
          "content": { "$ref": "#/$defs/untrusted_text" } } } } } } } } } }
```

**Cursor over `run_event` (FR-TEL-01).** Each digest call reads `run_event` rows with `(ts, id)` strictly after the
position named by `since_cursor`, up to a bounded scan budget (proposed target: 10 000 events per call). The returned
`cursor` is the last `run_event.id` (`evt_` plus 26 Crockford base32 characters) included in that scan. A repeated call
with the same
`since_cursor` after no new events returns an empty delta and the same cursor. Events before retention GC are not
replayed; a cursor older than retention behaves like an absent cursor (full snapshot within retention). Digest assembly
is scoped to projects the caller may use; other clients' runs never appear.

**Agent text.** Any `result.summary` (or other agent-written field included later) is copied into `untrusted_summaries`
as `untrusted_text`, never as authoritative instruction text (FR-CTL-02).

**Implemented (T14.3, Delegated, not owner-approved).** `src/mcp/digest.ts` assembles the digest read-only and
`src/mcp/server.ts` scopes it as `get_run`. Choices this section leaves open:

- The scan reads only the events of the caller's own visible runs (ledger read helpers `getRunEvent` and
  `listRunEventsForClient`, no migration), so the cursor never names or advances over another client's event. A
  `since_cursor` that is not the id of such an event is `INVALID_REQUEST` (`details.field = "since_cursor"`):
  `run_event` has no delete, so there is no garbage-collected cursor to treat as absent.
- Delta: a run touched by the scan is listed in `needs_attention` when its flag (§3.4.5, the same `needsAttentionOf`)
  is active at read time, in `finished` / `failed` when the scan holds its `state_transition` to `SUCCEEDED` /
  `FAILED`, and its meta-session's `PENDING` approval in `pending_approvals`; only the projects of touched runs are
  listed. With nothing new, `projects` is empty and `cursor` equals `since_cursor`.
- Snapshot (no `since_cursor`): every project the caller may use plus the projects of its runs, with all current flags
  and approvals and the newest 50 finished and failed runs per project. The output `cursor` is the caller's newest
  event, or `null` when it has none (amendment to the output schema above, which cannot name "no event").
- Every section of a listed project is present. Runs without a project (workspace-path runs) are not in the digest.
- Agent text: `result.summary` is removed from digest envelopes and appears once per run in `untrusted_summaries`,
  capped at 500 UTF-16 code units with a trailing "…" and no split surrogate pair; `result.verification_summary` is
  removed as well (it stays on `get_run`).
- `budget.exhausted` reads the same `ControlPlaneFacts` port as §3.4.5 (no budgets exist before T14.4); `budget.caps`
  and `paused` are omitted, not reported as unset or false, until T14.4 and T14.6 persist them.
- `pending_approvals` lists `privileged_scope` approvals; `approval_digest` is the §3.4.8 SHA-256 over controller
  fields. Dynamic-project and push revisions join with T14.8.

#### 3.4.4 `steer_run`, pause and resume (FR-CTL-04, FR-CTL-05)

**`steer_run`**

```jsonc
{ "input": { "type": "object", "required": ["run_id","note"], "properties": {
    "run_id": { "type": "string" },
    "note": { "type": "string", "minLength": 1, "maxLength": 4096 } } },
  "output": { "$ref": "#/$defs/envelope" } }
```

The note is recorded as a controller `steer_requested` `run_event` (bounded payload: length and hash only in the event;
full text in a controller-owned artifact if needed, never in MCP output). Delivery: at the next adapter boundary, live
input when the adapter declares `liveInput: true` in §2; otherwise stop at a boundary and continue the same native
session with the note (FR-SES-03, BR-7). Unknown run or a run the caller cannot see → `INVALID_REQUEST` or
`PERMISSION_BLOCKED` as for `get_run`. Terminal runs → no-op returning the terminal envelope.

**`pause_project` / `resume_project`**

```jsonc
{ "input": { "type": "object", "required": ["project_id"], "properties": {
    "project_id": { "type": "string" } } },
  "output": { "type": "object", "required": ["project_id","paused"], "properties": {
    "project_id": { "type": "string" },
    "paused": { "type": "boolean" } } } }
```

Only projects the caller may use; another client's project → `PERMISSION_BLOCKED`. Pause sets a ledger flag (§3.4.7);
admission checks it before QUEUED (running runs finish). **Operator CLI (global).** `switchboard pause` and
`switchboard resume` (names Proposed) set `control_plane.global_paused` (§3.4.7). While global pause is on, no project
admits new runs. Effect on an already-running `serve` daemon: the CLI writes an append-only `operator_pause_requested`
`run_event` on a synthetic control record (or a dedicated `control_plane_event` table, T14.6 chooses one shape); the
daemon applies it on its operator poll, same pattern as `approve` (§3.1). A paused project or global pause is visible in
`get_digest` (`paused: true`).

#### 3.4.5 Needs-attention derivation (FR-CTL-03)

Closed reason list (exactly these five values):

| Reason | Derived when (ledger facts only) |
| --- | --- |
| `awaiting_approval` | Run `status` is `AWAITING_APPROVAL`, or a `PENDING` approval row exists for a run of this meta-session the caller may see |
| `parked_loop` | Meta-session flag `parked_loop` is set (§3.4.6); the newest run shows this reason |
| `failed_after_retry` | Run `FAILED` and `attempt` equals `control_plane.loop_breaker.max_successors_per_meta_session` or the meta-session is parked |
| `stalled` | Run non-terminal, `RUNNING` or `LEASED`, and no `run_event` for this run in the last *T* minutes (§3.4.6); run is **not** cancelled |
| `budget_exhausted` | Project (or global) budget refusal is active for admission (§3.4.6) |

Multiple reasons may apply; the envelope and digest expose the highest-priority reason only (proposed order:
`budget_exhausted`, `parked_loop`, `awaiting_approval`, `stalled`, `failed_after_retry`). Priority is **Proposed** for
stable UI; T14.2 may adjust with tests.

**Implemented (T14.2, Delegated, not owner-approved).** `src/control/needs-attention.ts` derives the flag on read and
keeps the order above (`NEEDS_ATTENTION_PRIORITY`); `get_run` and `list_runs` attach it to the envelope, and the digest
(T14.3) is to call the same `needsAttentionOf`. Choices the table leaves open: `failed_after_retry` matches
`attempt >= max_successors_per_meta_session`; `budget_exhausted` attaches to the non-terminal runs of a resolved project
only; until the `control_plane.loop_breaker` keys exist, *T* and the successor cap use the §3.4.6 Assumption defaults
(30 and 8). `meta_session.parked_loop` and `control_plane_state` are read through a `ControlPlaneFacts` port whose default
reports neither (missing persistence infers nothing), so T14.4 and T14.5 wire their readers in without changing the
derivation. An unevaluable stall timestamp does not flag `stalled`; an approval lookup that breaks the
one-approval-per-meta-session invariant shows `awaiting_approval`.

#### 3.4.6 Budgets and loop breaker (FR-CTL-06, FR-CTL-07)

**Configuration keys** (operator values; never presented as provider limits, ADR-0007). Validated at config load; unset
cap means unlimited for that dimension.

| Key | Type | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `control_plane.budgets.global.runs_per_day` | integer ≥ 0 or absent | Admission count per UTC day, all projects |
| `control_plane.budgets.global.concurrent_runs` | integer ≥ 0 or absent | Non-terminal runs at once |
| `control_plane.budgets.global.wall_clock_per_run_seconds` | integer ≥ 0 or absent | Wall clock from `LEASED` to terminal |
| `control_plane.budgets.global.usage_per_window` | object or absent | `{ "window_seconds", "max_reported_tokens" }` over provider-reported usage in the window |
| `control_plane.budgets.projects.<id>.*` | same shape | Overrides for one project id |
| `control_plane.loop_breaker.identical_failure_threshold` | integer ≥ 2 | **N**: same `failure_code` or same failing verification name *N* times in one meta-session → park |
| `control_plane.loop_breaker.stall_minutes` | integer ≥ 1 | **T**: minutes without a `run_event` on a non-terminal run → `stalled` flag only |
| `control_plane.loop_breaker.max_successors_per_meta_session` | integer ≥ 1 | Cap on successor runs (§16.5 and quota failover successors count toward the same total) |

**Assumption defaults** (operator tuning; not provider facts): **N** = 3, **T** = 30 minutes, **successor cap** = 8.

On budget refusal at admission, the controller records `budget_refused` on the project (ledger or `project_event`) and
sets `needs_attention` reason `budget_exhausted` until the window resets or the operator raises the cap. `get_digest`
reports `budget.exhausted` and per-cap `used`/`limit` with `limit: null` when unset.

On the *N*th identical failure in a meta-session, the controller sets `meta_session.parked_loop = true` (Proposed
column or side table), admits no further successors, and surfaces `parked_loop`. Stall detection uses the injectable
clock (tests); it never cancels the run.

#### 3.4.7 Pause and budget persistence (Proposed)

```sql
CREATE TABLE control_plane_state (      -- migration T14.6; one row per project_id or '*' for global
  project_id    TEXT PRIMARY KEY,       -- configured or dynamic project id, or '*' for global pause
  paused        INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (paused IN (0,1)),
  paused_by     TEXT,                   -- mcp:<client_id> or os user for CLI
  paused_at     TEXT,
  budget_flags_json TEXT CHECK (budget_flags_json IS NULL OR json_valid(budget_flags_json))
) STRICT;
```

`meta_session` gains `parked_loop INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` (T14.5). `approval.decided_by` accepts
`mcp_proxy:<client_id>` (T14.8, ADR-0047); no separate `decided_via` column.

#### 3.4.8 Approval proxy tools (FR-CTL-08, ADR-0047)

Offered in `tools/list` only when `mcp_clients[].approval_proxy` is true (default false). Set only in operator
configuration; never over MCP. Optional `mcp_clients[].approval_proxy_targets`: string array of client ids (default: the
client itself).

**`list_pending_approvals`**

```jsonc
{ "input": { "type": "object", "properties": {
    "project_id": { "type": "string" } } },
  "output": { "type": "object", "required": ["approvals"], "properties": {
    "approvals": { "type": "array", "items": { "$ref": "#/$defs/approval_proxy_item" } } } } }
```

Lists `PENDING` approvals for listed target clients only. `approval_digest` = SHA-256 over a canonical JSON object:
`{ "v": 1, "approval_id", "kind", "scope_hash" | "revision_hash", "target_client_id", "requested_at" }` (privileged
uses §3.1 `scope_hash`; dynamic project uses ADR-0030 revision hash). Descriptions are controller-generated fields only
(ADR-0047 item 2d).

**`decide_approval`**

```jsonc
{ "input": { "type": "object", "required": ["approval_id","approval_digest","decision"], "properties": {
    "approval_id": { "type": "string" },
    "approval_digest": { "type": "string", "pattern": "^[0-9a-f]{64}$" },
    "decision": { "enum": ["approve","deny"] } } },
  "output": { "type": "object", "required": ["approval_id","status"], "properties": {
    "approval_id": { "type": "string" },
    "status": { "enum": ["APPROVED","DENIED"] } } } }
```

Fail closed: wrong digest, stale id, target client not in `approval_proxy_targets`, or row not `PENDING` →
`INVALID_REQUEST`, nothing written. On success, same transitions as CLI `approve`/`deny` (§3.1), with `decided_by =
"mcp_proxy:<proxy_client_id>"` and a `approval_decided` `run_event`. Each success triggers sink delivery
(§3.4.9). Clients without `approval_proxy` do not see these tools (absent from `tools/list`, not error at list time).

*Amends §3.1 **Approve**:* except for `approval_proxy` clients using §3.4.8, no MCP tool approves.

#### 3.4.9 Notification sinks (FR-CTL-10, ADR-0048)

**Configuration** (`notification_sinks[]`, off when absent):

| Field | Type | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | string | Operator-chosen label |
| `kind` | `webhook` \| `ntfy` | Delivery mechanism |
| `url` | string (https) | Webhook URL (operator may point this at the Grok bot's deployment-generated webhook; no Grok-specific code) |
| `ntfy` | object | For `kind: ntfy`: `{ "server", "topic", "token"?: string }`; publish uses ntfy's documented HTTP publish API ([ntfy publish](https://docs.ntfy.sh/publish/), cited here per owner decision 53) |
| `events` | array of `sink_event_kind` | Filter; default all kinds |
| `max_per_minute` | integer ≥ 1 | Rate limit; excess dropped and counted |

**Payload** is exactly `notification_payload` (§3.4.1). **Link** is an operator-configured base URL plus stable path
(`/digest` or `/runs/<run_id>` on the MCP HTTP `public_url` when set, else a placeholder `switchboard://run/<run_id>` for
stdio-only deployments). Delivery is asynchronous, bounded timeout (proposed target: 5 s), HTTPS with verification;
failures append `sink_delivery_failed` counts to the ledger without payload or URL (NFR-SEC-01). Never blocks admission
or run execution.

**Implementation (T14.9, `src/control/sinks.ts`, `src/config/notification-sinks.ts`; Delegated, not owner-approved):**

- `url` and `ntfy.server` must be `https://` with no userinfo, query or fragment; plain HTTP is refused at load, also
  for loopback. `ntfy.topic` matches `^[-_A-Za-z0-9]{1,64}$`. At most 8 sinks with unique `id`s (**Assumption**).
  `max_per_minute` defaults to 10 and is at most 600 (**Assumption**; a sliding 60 s window per sink). Config errors
  name the key and never echo a configured value.
- Reason codes per `event_kind`: `needs_attention` takes a `needs_attention_reason`; `approval_pending` and
  `approval_decided` an `approval_kind`; `run_terminal` the terminal state (`SUCCEEDED`, `FAILED`, `CANCELLED`);
  `budget_exhausted` the literal `budget_exhausted`; `project_paused` `project_paused` or `global_paused`. `project_id`
  is a project id, `-` (a run admitted without a project) or `*` (all projects). An event with any other value, or a
  `run_id` that is not `run_` + ULID, is dropped, not sent.
- Link: the origin of `public_url` plus `/runs/<run_id>` or `/digest`; else `switchboard://run/<run_id>` or
  `switchboard://digest`.
- Webhook: `POST` to `url`, `Content-Type: application/json`, body exactly `notification_payload`. ntfy: JSON publish
  ([ntfy publish](https://docs.ntfy.sh/publish/), "Publish as JSON") to the server root with body `{ topic, title:
  "Switchboard: <event_kind>", message: "project_id=<id> reason_code=<code> run_id=<id|none>", click: <link>, tags:
  ["switchboard"] }` and `Authorization: Bearer <token>` when `token` is set.
- Delivery starts on a later event-loop turn than the transition, uses no connection reuse, follows no redirect and
  times out after 5 s. Outcome codes: `timeout`, `transport_error`, `http_status` (with the status) and
  `rate_limited`. A failure or drop for an event with a run appends a `sink_delivery_failed` run event `{ sink_id,
  event_kind, reason_code[, status] }` and logs the same fields; an error's own text is never used.
- Producers in T14.9: a run reaching a terminal state (`run_terminal`) and an operator approval of a privileged run
  (FRD T3, `approval_decided` / `privileged_scope`), from the lifecycle's existing transition hook in `serve`. The other
  kinds are emitted by their own tasks (T14.2, T14.4, T14.6, T14.8) through the same dispatcher.

#### 3.4.10 `tool_profile` (FR-CTL-11)

`mcp_clients[].tool_profile`: `orchestrator` (default) or `console`. `tools/list` returns:

| Profile | Tools |
| --- | --- |
| `orchestrator` | Today's full set (unchanged) |
| `console` | `get_digest`, `list_runs`, `get_run`, `delegate_task`, `steer_run`, `pause_project`, `resume_project` |

Approval-proxy tools are added to the list only when `approval_proxy` is true, regardless of profile. `console` is the
first target for the Grok bot (owner decision 55); orchestrator profile stays for primary coding agents.

## 4. Failure taxonomy

`failure_code` on a `FAILED` run holds exactly one of these codes. `ELIGIBILITY_NONE` and `INVALID_REQUEST` were **added
during planning** (not in [R2]'s list). Retryability follows FR-CAP-05: only `RATE_LIMIT_TRANSIENT`,
`PROVIDER_OVERLOADED` and `PROCESS_CRASH` before any workspace mutation retry automatically; rollback-or-resume instead
of replay follows FR-WS-04.

| Code | Meaning | Retryable? | Workspace action | Typical sources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `AUTH_REQUIRED` | Provider not logged in; operator must log in natively | No | None (no mutation expected); report login command | `authPreflight`; headless auth error (FR-AUTH-02) |
| `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` | Operation denied by profile/permission rules, or caller above its max profile | No | Roll back if mutated | Provider permission-denied event; MCP caller check (FR-MCP-06, FR-POL-05) |
| `SANDBOX_VIOLATION` | Sandbox blocked an action | No | Roll back if mutated; flag for operator review | Provider sandbox event; outer sandbox denial (FR-POL-05) |
| `MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` | Requested model not present or not entitled | No (re-route may succeed) | None | Provider error category; model registry miss |
| `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED` | Subscription limit hit | No automatic retry; fail over, queue until known reset, or return (FR-CAP-06) | Roll back if mutated | Provider-reported limit; billing error category |
| `RATE_LIMIT_TRANSIENT` | Short-term throttle | Yes, if workspace unchanged | None if clean; else rollback or resume | Rate-limit error event; 429-like category |
| `PROVIDER_OVERLOADED` | Provider-side overload/server error | Yes, if workspace unchanged | As above | `overloaded`/server error categories |
| `TOOL_FAILURE` | Agent tool or command failed in a way the run cannot continue | No | Keep result if checkpointed, else roll back | Provider tool error; non-zero exit with error event |
| `TEST_FAILURE` | Verification commands failed after the agent finished | No | Keep post-run checkpoint (work product), mark failed verification | Verification stage (FR-EXE-08, M3) |
| `PROCESS_TIMEOUT` | Wall-clock or idle timeout; process group killed | No | Assess mutation; roll back or keep as unverified | Supervisor timers (FR-EXE-01) |
| `PROCESS_CRASH` | Provider process died abnormally, or controller restart orphaned it (cause `controller_restart`, FR-EXE-06) | Yes only if no mutation since pre-run checkpoint | Mutation check before any action | Signal/exit; reconciliation |
| `OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR` | Unparseable, oversize-line or unexpected-protocol output | No | Assess mutation; roll back if changed | Parser; line limit (§8) |
| `ADAPTER_VERSION_UNVERIFIED` | Installed version outside the validated range, or unknown or unparseable (a missing manifest is `ELIGIBILITY_NONE`, [FRD BR-3](../requirements/FRD.md#3-business-rules)) | No | None | Probe; manifest check (FR-ADP-03, FR-POL-07) |
| `CANCELLED` | Operator or caller cancelled | No | Roll back to pre-run checkpoint unless operator keeps partial work (Proposed) | `cancel_run`, WF-4 |
| `ELIGIBILITY_NONE` *(added during planning)* | No provider passes hard eligibility for this request | No (state may change) | None | Router (FR-RTE-01, FR-MCP-05) |
| `INVALID_REQUEST` *(added during planning)* | Schema, path, size or idempotency validation failed | No | None | MCP validation (FR-MCP-09, FR-POL-10) |

`CANCELLED` is also a run *state* (FRD); the code is used in error envelopes and `run_event` payloads, while a cancelled
run has `state = 'CANCELLED'` and `failure_code` NULL per the DDL check.

A handoff bundle that cannot be built (M5) is reported as `TOOL_FAILURE` with `details.reason =
"HANDOFF_BUNDLE_UNAVAILABLE"` before any run exists ([§15.7](#157-continue_task-with-handoff),
[ADR-0038](../adr/0038-handoff-bundle-and-continuity.md)); no code is added for it.

Inner-agent failures (unreachable, timeout, invalid output, open circuit, suspension) are **not** run failure codes: they
never fail a run, never appear in `run.failure_code` and never feed retry (FR-INN-06, FR-INN-08); they are recorded as
`inner_call` outcomes and `inner_*` run events ([§13.8](#138-telemetry-and-events)).

## 5. Workspace and lease algorithm

Implements FR-WS-01..06 and [ADR-0006](../adr/0006-worktree-checkpoints-no-blind-replay.md). All Git commands use
`execFile('git', args)` with a scrubbed environment, `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null` and `--no-verify` on commits
(Proposed, see [TDD §3.4](TDD.md#34-security-engineering)); porcelain/`-z` output only.

**Naming.** Worktree path `<data_dir>/worktrees/<sess>/<run>`; branch `switchboard/<sess>/<run>`. One controller branch
per run, created from the previous head, so history per meta-session is a linear chain of run branches (Proposed).
`inspect-untrusted` on a non-Git directory uses no worktree and read-only access (FR-WS-06).
*M5 (Proposed, Delegated, [ADR-0038](../adr/0038-handoff-bundle-and-continuity.md)):* a native resume whose adapter
does not declare `resumeCwd: 'independent'` runs in the worktree path of the run that created the native session, with
its own new branch at the head checkpoint ([§15.6](#156-working-directory-of-a-native-resume)); paths stay under
`<data_dir>/worktrees/<sess>/` and are never shared across meta-sessions (FR-WS-08).

**Lease (one writer per meta-session chain, Proposed).** Because each run branches from the session head, two concurrent
writers would fork; the lease lives on `meta_session` (`lease_run_id`, `lease_expires_at`). Parallel tasks use separate
meta-sessions and therefore separate worktrees (FR-WS-02). Concurrent runs of one project each get their own worktree
and lease whichever meta-session they belong to (FR-WS-08, owner decision 42, Delegated). Parallel subtasks inside one
session: to be detailed in M5.

*As built (T9.11, 2026-10-13, Delegated).* FR-WS-08 needed no change to the lease or worktree model: a run's worktree
and branch are keyed by meta-session and run (`<data_dir>/worktrees/<sess>/<run>`, `switchboard/<sess>/<run>`), the
lease is one column pair per `meta_session` row, and a meta-session has at most one non-terminal run (FR-SES-06), so
two concurrent runs of one project are always in different meta-sessions with their own worktree, branch and lease;
nothing is keyed by project or context. The one rule that holds per worktree (FR-WS-02, at most one write lease) is
unchanged. One defect was found and fixed: another run's ref check could see a checkpoint commit before its `post` row
(see [§12.5](#125-ref-snapshot-and-violation-handling), "Concurrent runs"); finalization is now serialised per
repository root. Tests: `src/lifecycle/concurrent-worktrees.test.ts`. Not built: the `container` clause of FR-WS-08
(M10, FR-PRJ-05) and FR-WS-09 (Proposed, not accepted, Q-55; its tests are skipped placeholders).

*As built (worktree creation, 2026-10-13, Delegated).* Git takes no lock on `.git/worktrees/` during `git worktree add`,
so concurrent preparations on one repository failed intermittently: an add that enumerated worktrees read a sibling's
half-created admin directory (`fatal: failed to read .git/worktrees/<id>/commondir: Success`), or a sibling's
`git worktree prune` removed it (`fatal: could not open '.git/worktrees/<id>/locked' for writing`). The partial-worktree
repair, the directory creation and `git worktree add` now run one at a time per repository root in the process
(`src/workspace/manager.ts`, `serialisedWorktreeCreate`); no retry. Test: "creates worktrees for concurrent runs on one
repository one at a time (T9.11)" in `src/workspace/workspace.test.ts`.

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
  participant S as Scheduler
  participant L as Ledger txn
  participant W as Workspace Manager
  participant X as Supervisor
  S->>L: BEGIN IMMEDIATE; check lease free or expired; set lease_run_id, expires_at; run -> LEASED; event
  L-->>S: COMMIT
  S->>W: resolve base (session head); write 'pre' checkpoint row (intent)
  W->>W: git worktree add -b branch path base; record pre checkpoint
  S->>X: spawn (run -> RUNNING); heartbeat extends lease_expires_at
  X-->>S: exit + events
  S->>W: detect mutation, commit post checkpoint, write diff artifact
  S->>L: run -> FINALIZING/terminal; release lease; head_commit = post; event
```

**Acquire.** In one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction: if `lease_run_id IS NULL` or `lease_expires_at < now`, set both and
transition the run to `LEASED` with an event; otherwise the run stays queued. Lease TTL default 2 x wall-clock timeout,
extended by a heartbeat while the supervisor holds the process (proposed target; values in §8). An expired lease whose
holder run is non-terminal is resolved by reconciliation, not by simply stealing it: the stale run is first marked
`PROCESS_CRASH` (cause `lease_expired`) and its worktree assessed.

**Base and pre-run checkpoint.** Base = `meta_session.head_commit`, or for a new session the repository `HEAD` of the
allowed workspace (read via `git rev-parse`; the operator's checked-out branch and working tree are never modified,
FR-WS-05). A `base` row is written to `workspace_checkpoint`, then `git worktree add -b <branch> <path> <commit>`. The
`pre` checkpoint equals the base commit of the worktree; no commit is made at this point because the worktree starts
clean.

**Post-run checkpoint (FR-WS-03).** After the process exits and verification (if any) ends: `git add -A` in the worktree
(hooks disabled), `git commit` with a controller identity and the run ID in the message, record `post` checkpoint row,
write `diff` (`git diff --binary <pre>..<post>`) and `changed_files` artifacts. Commit is skipped if the tree is clean.

**Mutation detection.** Before any retry, rollback or resume decision, compare worktree to the `pre` commit: `git status
--porcelain=v2 -z --untracked-files=all` plus `git diff --quiet <pre>` and `HEAD == pre`. Any difference, a changed
`HEAD` or an unreadable worktree counts as *mutated* (fail closed, NFR-SEC-06). Known limits: ignored files are not
tracked by default (Proposed: also check with `--ignored` for the profile's high-risk patterns) and writes outside the
worktree are out of Git's sight; the sandbox, not detection, bounds those.

**Failure handling (FR-WS-04).** Unchanged worktree: eligible for retry per §9. Mutated: either `git reset --hard <pre>
&& git clean -fd` in the worktree (rollback; the failed attempt's diff is saved as an artifact first), or native resume
of the provider session on the same worktree, which counts as a continuation and never replays the original request.
Choice is policy-driven; default rollback for `TOOL_FAILURE`/`OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR`, resume only when the adapter
reports a resumable session (Proposed).

*As built in M1 (FRD §4.4 owns the outcome, so it wins over the destructive reset above):* the run lifecycle commits the
failed attempt's state to the run's own branch as an evidence checkpoint, stores the diff artifact, and moves the
meta-session head back to the pre-run checkpoint; it does not run `git reset --hard` / `git clean` on the evidence.
`WorkspaceManager.rollback` exists in `src/workspace/` but the lifecycle follows FRD §4.4. The reset-based wording above
is superseded for the lifecycle path.

**Push and operator tree.** The workspace manager never runs `git push`, touches no remote and writes nothing to the
operator's working tree or checked-out branch; the result is the run branch plus diff artifact (FR-WS-05). Publication
is a separate, policy-gated path owned by COMP-GIT ([§12](#12-injected-git-tools-commits-and-publication)). Checkpoint
commits here carry the controller identity and the run ID in their message, which is acceptable only because they are
internal: they are never pushed and never part of the publish ref (FR-GIT-08). *As built in M1* the checkpoints are
made on the run branch `switchboard/<sess>/<run>` with the identity in `src/workspace/git.ts`; M9 must not change
that, only add the publish ref beside it.

**GC (FR-WS-07, NFR-DATA-01, M3; to be detailed in M3).** Retention job deletes, per expired terminal run, in one
logical unit: worktree directory (`git worktree remove --force`, then `git worktree prune`), controller branch,
artifact files (set `deleted_at`). Never touches branches outside `switchboard/`. *Proposed (FR-WS-09, owner decision
42):* a concurrent run's worktree may also be removed on merge or abandon before retention; the branch and
artifacts stay until GC. From M9 the publish refs (§12.3) are
collected with the rest of their meta-session. Orphan sweep: worktree directories or `switchboard/*` branches with no
matching `run` row are reported by `doctor` and removed only by GC after the retention period.

*As built (T3.12, Delegated decision 28 under owner decision 25):* the unit is a whole idle meta-session
([Q-18](../ROADMAP.md#73-open-questions-for-zemdregon-not-blocking-m2-unless-noted), `src/workspace/gc.ts`; `run_event`
rows are kept, Q-19). It is wired three ways, none with a new config key. `doctor` reports what `retention.days`
(default 30) would collect and the orphan sweep, read-only (`findOrphans`, `planRetentionGc`). `switchboard gc` lists
the same by default (dry run); `--apply` collects, needs `retention.days` set in the config and the data-directory
lock (so it is refused while `serve` runs). `serve` runs one pass after reconciliation, before the lifecycle starts or
MCP accepts calls, and then every `retention.interval`, only when `retention.days` is set: unset means nothing is
collected. Orphans are reported but not yet removed. The sweep does not count a publish ref
(`refs/heads/<prefix><sess>`, §12.3) of a meta-session that has a ledger row as an orphan; one whose session is
unknown is still reported. GC marks first (R-42, decision 28): one transaction re-checks idleness, sets the
meta-session `ARCHIVED` and appends the intent event, and admission refuses an `ARCHIVED` meta-session with
`INVALID_REQUEST`; a marked, unfinished session is a candidate again and rolls forward.

**Crash safety.** Order is intent row (with the `ARCHIVED` mark, one transaction), then Git action, then outcome
row. On startup reconciliation (WF-8) a run in
`LEASED`/`RUNNING` with a worktree but no outcome is assessed by the mutation check; a lease row is cleared only after
its run reaches a terminal state. `git worktree add` interrupted mid-way is detected by `git worktree list` disagreement
