author and committer variables) and `input` (standard input). Choices where §12.3 was silent:

- **Publish ref.** `refs/heads/switchboard/pub/<sess>` by default, not `<push.branch_prefix><sess>`: the example prefix
  `switchboard/` would give `refs/heads/switchboard/<sess>`, which cannot coexist with the run branch
  `refs/heads/switchboard/<sess>/<run>`. `push.branch_prefix` stays the remote branch prefix (§12.6). A prefix in the
  `switchboard/sess_*` namespace is refused. Open: Q-42.
- **Order (replaces "move the ref, then insert" for crash safety).** (1) temporary index seeded from the publish head
  (or the
  session's `base_commit`), `add`, `write-tree`, `NOTHING_TO_COMMIT` if the tree equals the parent's; (2) sanitise;
  (3) `commit-tree` (identity and dates from the context, message on standard input, trailing newline only); (4) lineage
  check: `rev-list --count <new> --not <base> <old head>` must be 1; (5) append the `git_commit_intent` run event,
  which holds
  the whole provenance record (SHAs, ids, provider, harness, model, rule counts; no message text); (6) `update-ref
  <ref> <new>
  <old>` (old is all zeros for the first commit); on failure re-read the ref: moved means `git_commit_aborted` and a retry
  from step 1, once, then `PUBLISH_CONFLICT`; (7) in one transaction append the `git_commit` event and insert the
  `git_commit` row. Failure to write step 7 after a successful step 6 is reported as `PROVENANCE_PENDING` with the SHA.
- **Recovery.** `GitPublisher.recover()` (call at controller start, before serving; wiring is T9.4) finds `git_commit_intent`
  events with neither a `git_commit` row nor a `git_commit_aborted` event for the same SHA. If the commit is an
  ancestor of
  the publish ref it writes the row and event (the event payload has `recovered: true`); if not it appends
  `git_commit_aborted` (`not_published_at_recovery`); if the repository cannot be read it reports the SHA as `unresolved`
  and tries again next time.
- **Paths.** With `paths`, each is normalised and checked before anything is staged; any refusal refuses the whole
  call and
  reports every refused path with a reason (`invalid`, `outside_worktree`, `git_dir`, `symlink_in_path`, `missing`,
  `ignored`, `nested_repository`, `contains_nested_repository`); the call code is `PATH_OUTSIDE_WORKTREE` when any
  path is
  outside or reached through a symbolic link, else `PATH_REFUSED`. A path must be worktree-relative (an absolute path is
  `outside_worktree`); `.` is `invalid`. A missing path is a deletion only if the parent tree has it. An ignored path is
  refused unless the parent tree already tracks it. A directory path that contains a nested repository (ADR-0022) is
  refused: name the files. Staging uses `git --literal-pathspecs add -- <paths>`; names starting with `-` or `:` were
  tested. Without `paths`, `git add -A -- . <nested excludes>` stages every change; ignored files are skipped.
- **Not tested here.** Clean and smudge filters, submodules, very large path sets (the limit is 256), Git older than 2.43.0
  and concurrent publishers in separate processes (only the compare-and-swap protects them).

**As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.7, finalize-only mode, FR-GIT-09, FRD BR-30; [decision
38](../ROADMAP.md#42-owner-decisions); owner text above unchanged).** Code: `src/git/finalize.ts`, called from
`RunLifecycle.finalize` (`src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts`) with a second `GitPublisher` whose `allowedStates` is
`["FINALIZING"]` (`src/cli/serve.ts`); the tool channel's publisher still accepts only RUNNING. Choices where §12.3 and
BR-30 were silent:

- **When.** Only for an adapter whose `commitChannel` is not `injected`, a project run whose frozen snapshot has
  `finalize_commit: true`, and a worktree run. Order inside FINALIZING: ref check (§12.5), assessment and evidence
  checkpoint, then the commit, then the terminal transition. The steps of §12.3 apply unchanged except step 1, which
  checks FINALIZING instead of RUNNING (the run still holds the lease). The FRD §4.5 placement "ahead of the
  checkpoint" is read as "before the terminal transition": the commit takes its path list from the checkpoint's
  changed-file list, and neither touches the other's refs.
- **Input.** Paths: the changed-file list of the post-run checkpoint (pre to post, nested repositories excluded),
  deletions included; at most 256 (§12.1), else no commit (an approved bypass run has no count limit, decision 44).
  Message: the adapter's structured final summary
  (`RunResult.summary`, FR-ADP-04), passed whole to the §12.4 sanitiser with the run's native session ids; empty,
  absent or empty after sanitising means no commit; over 8192 characters means no commit (no truncation, so an
  identifier cannot be split past the sanitiser). *Approved bypass runs (ROADMAP decision 44, Q-45):* neither limit
  applies. The publisher receives `looseLimits: true` (honoured only with `origin: "finalize_only"`; the `commit` tool
  never sets it), skips its 256-path check, sanitises the whole message, and then cuts a message longer than 8192
  characters at a line boundary (or at the limit when no boundary keeps half of it) and appends a one-line note; path
  classification, the temporary index and the compare-and-swap are unchanged, with pathspecs passed on stdin. Identity,
  project and context: the frozen snapshot.
- **Never committed** (each a `git_finalize_commit` event `{outcome: "skipped", reason}`): the run is not about to end
  SUCCEEDED (`run_not_succeeded`); `environment_not_host` (M10); `context_not_writable`; `no_identity`; any
  `git_policy_violation` event in any run of the meta-session (`git_policy_violation`, BR-27); no publisher configured
  (`service_unavailable`); `no_changes`; `too_many_paths`; `no_message`; `message_too_long`; `nothing_to_commit`.
  A §12.3 error is `{outcome: "failed", reason: <code>}` with refused paths as counts per reason (`refused`), never
  names; `PROVENANCE_PENDING` adds the SHA. A commit is `{outcome: "committed", commit_sha, provenance_id,
  files_changed}`. The run's outcome never changes; the terminal transition payload gains `finalize_commit: <sha>` or
  `finalize_commit_skipped: <reason>`.
- **Provenance.** The T9.3 sequence unchanged (intent, compare-and-swap, event and row in one transaction;
  `recover()` resolves a crash), with `origin = 'finalize_only'`. The intent and the `git_commit` event add `via:
  "finalize"` and `finalizing_event_id` (the run's transition event into FINALIZING), which stands in for FR-GIT-07's
  tool-call event; `tool_event_id` keeps its T9.3 meaning (the `git_commit` event). No column or migration.
- **Bypass.** An approved bypass run is handled identically; nothing in this path reads the profile.

#### 12.3.1 Signing (FR-GIT-10)

With `sign: true` step 4 asks Git to sign the commit object using the operator's existing signing configuration
(`user.signingkey`, `gpg.format`, and the operator's own agent or keyring), run by the controller process with the
operator's environment, not the scrubbed one. The controller never reads, stores or logs the key, and never exports the
agent socket or `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`/`GPG_*` variables to the provider process (FR-EXE-07). Failure (no key, locked agent,
non-zero exit) returns `SIGNING_FAILED` to the agent and writes no commit. The BR-31 reach check applies: a context that
signs needs an environment in which the agent cannot reach the key. Which signing formats work in a headless controller
is verified in the M9 spike (T9.0); until then `sign` is accepted only if `projects validate` can produce a test
signature in a scratch repository.

**Note 2026-10-13 (T9.0 spike; Delegated).** Verified on Git 2.43.0 with GnuPG: `git -c user.signingkey=<fpr>
commit-tree <tree> -p <parent> -S -m signed` produced a commit with a `gpgsig` header and `git verify-commit` reported a
good signature, using a throwaway unprotected Ed25519 key generated in a temporary `GNUPGHOME` (no real key was used).
With `-c gpg.program=/nonexistent/gpg` the call exited 1 with `error: gpg failed to sign the data` and printed no commit
id, so a signing failure yields no commit, as `SIGNING_FAILED` requires. `gpg-agent` did not start with a `GNUPGHOME`
path longer than the socket limit (see §12.2). **Unverified:** SSH-format signing (`ssh-keygen` is not installed on this
host; the Git 2.34.0 release notes introduce it and warn against OpenSSH 8.7), X.509, passphrase-protected or hardware
keys, and signing with the operator's real agent. Only the unprotected-key GnuPG case counts as headless-verified.

The exact Git plumbing is fixed in the M9 spike. Nothing here touches the run branch, the worktree's index or the
operator's checkout.

**As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.10, [ADR-0034](../adr/0034-controller-side-commit-signing.md); owner text above
unchanged).** Code: `src/git/sign.ts`, `GitPublisher.commit` (`src/git/commit.ts`, input `sign`),
`evaluateSigningReach` (`src/git/credential-reach.ts`), the channel and finalize-only callers (`src/git/channel.ts`,
`src/git/finalize.ts`, `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts`), the snapshot field `project.sign` (`src/mcp/server.ts`) and
`projects validate` (`src/cli/projects.ts`). Choices where this section was silent, and where it differs:

- **Environment.** Not "the operator's environment" wholesale: an allowlist (`SIGNING_ENV_ALLOWLIST`: `PATH`, `HOME`,
  `USER`, `LOGNAME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, `GNUPGHOME`, `GPG_AGENT_INFO`, `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`) plus
  `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and `LC_ALL=C`, given only to the controller's own `git commit-tree -S` child. No inherited
  `GIT_*`. Own session, message on standard input, 60 s timeout (Proposed) killing the process group, hooks off at
  command scope, standard error discarded. The provider process keeps its adapter allowlist (FR-EXE-07), which names
  none of the agent variables.
- **Configuration.** The operator's global and system configuration only. `commit-tree -S` runs with `GIT_DIR` set to a
  scratch bare repository the controller creates and removes, and `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY` set to the repository's object
  store, so the repository's own configuration is never read for signing. Separately, a repository whose local or
  worktree configuration (or an included file) sets `gpg.*` or `user.signingkey` is refused (`repo_config_unsafe`),
  mirroring `REPO_CONFIG_UNSAFE` (§12.6). Limitation: an operator `includeIf "gitdir:..."` keyed on the repository path
  does not match the scratch `GIT_DIR`.
- **BR-31.** `evaluateSigningReach`: the push rule's environment, bypass and shell checks (same code), without the
  push-policy precondition; the owner decision 37 exemption for a controller-verified approved bypass run applies
  (Delegated reading, Q-56). Evaluated at each `commit` call (agent tool) and at FINALIZING (finalize-only).
- **Order and failure.** Signing checks (BR-31, repository keys) come after the run, lease and worktree checks and
  before the tree is built; the signed `commit-tree` replaces the plain one in step 3 of the T9.3 order. Any refusal,
  non-zero exit, timeout or missing `gpgsig`/`gpgsig-sha256` header throws `SIGNING_FAILED` (agent message: a fixed
  text with the reason code `credential_reach`, `repo_config_unsafe`, `sign_failed`, `timeout` or
  `signature_missing`) and appends `git_commit_signing_failed` `{origin, reason, credential_reach?}`, before any
  `git_commit_intent`: the compare-and-swap never runs, the publish ref is unchanged, nothing is committed unsigned.
  Finalize-only records `{outcome: "failed", reason: "SIGNING_FAILED"}`. A signed commit adds `signed: true` to the
  intent and the `git_commit` event; the commit differs from the unsigned one only by Git's signature header.
- **Snapshot and configuration.** `project.sign: true` is frozen at admission (absent when off, so unsigned runs keep
  the same snapshot and code path). The loader refuses `sign: true` without an `identity` or outside the primary
  writable context.
- **`projects validate`.** For a context with `sign: true` that passes the filesystem checks: `SIGNING_CONFIG_UNSAFE`
  for repository signing keys, then one test signature of an empty root commit in a throwaway scratch repository with
  the command's environment (`SIGNING_UNAVAILABLE` with the reason code on failure). Not run at admission or for
  dynamic projects (their `sign` waits for `projects approve`, ADR-0030); every signed commit fails closed at run time.
- **Not verified.** Only GnuPG with a throwaway unprotected key in a temporary `GNUPGHOME` (Git 2.43.0, GnuPG 2.4.4).
  SSH-format, X.509, passphrase-protected and hardware keys and the operator's real agent are unverified; no live
  provider was run.

### 12.4 Message sanitisation

Line-oriented. The built-in rules are illustrative starting points and **must be tested against fixtures** of typical agent
output before M9 is Done; operator patterns are added through `git.sanitize_patterns` (JavaScript regular-expression
sources, case-insensitive, validated at config load with a length limit; Proposed).

| Rule ID | Removes | Basis |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `trailer.co-author` | Any line starting `Co-authored-by:` | zemdregon's decision: no co-author trailers |
| `trailer.generated` | Lines starting `Generated-by:`, `Assisted-by:`, `Generated-with:` or `Assisted-with:` | Same; key list Proposed |
| `footer.generated-by` | Lines of the form "generated with/by ..." that name an AI tool, assistant or a provider or product from this project's provider list or Switchboard | Same |
| `id.controller` | Any token matching the controller's own ID formats (`sess_` or `run_` plus 26 ULID characters, [§3.0](#30-shared-types)) | Session IDs must not leak |
| `id.native-session` | The run's own provider-native session ID, as read from structured output (FR-ADP-04), wherever it appears | Same; exact-match, so no guessing |
| `url.session` | URLs whose path or query contains a session or conversation identifier of this run | Same |

After removal, runs of blank lines collapse and leading and trailing blank lines are trimmed. The controller never
adds a line. This holds for text drafted by the inner agent and for the PR draft ([§13.6](#136-task-contracts)): they
pass the same rules, with no inner-specific exceptions and no inner influence on the rule set (FR-INN-06). The removed
text is **not** recorded: `sanitizer_json` holds only `{ "rules": { "<rule id>": <count> }
}`. The rule set cannot catch what it does not describe, and it does not touch file contents (risk R-14).

**As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.3, ADR-0025).** Code: `src/git/sanitize.ts`, `src/git/safe-pattern.ts`. The table
above is implemented with these additions: rule `trailer.session` (a vendor-prefixed `-Session`/`-Conversation` key
such as
`Claude-Session:`, `Session-Id:`/`Run-Id:` forms, and any trailer whose key starts with a provider name and a hyphen),
`trailer.ai-signoff` (`Signed-off-by:`, `Reviewed-by:` and similar naming an AI product), `id.controller` over every ID
prefix of §1 (`sess run evt art dec ckp rte apv adv use pbd gcm gps` plus 26 or more characters), `id.native-session` for
ids of at least 6 characters, and `url.session` (markdown links and bare URLs whose path or query holds a session,
conversation or thread id, or that are session pages on `claude.ai`, `chatgpt.com` and similar hosts; documentation links
on those hosts stay). Line rules match an NFKC copy of the line with zero-width and bidirectional control characters
removed, so `Ｃｏ－ａｕｔｈｏｒｅｄ－ｂｙ：` and `Co\u200b-authored-by:` are caught. `footer.generated-by` needs a verb
(`generated created written authored made built assisted co-authored produced drafted implemented`) near the start of the
line, `with|by|using|via` within 80 characters, and a product or provider name anywhere on the line. Whole lines are
removed by line rules; token rules leave the surrounding text. The passes repeat until nothing more is removed (at most
four) and the sanitiser refuses (`SANITISER_FAILED`) a text it cannot make stable, a message over 65536 characters, or an
exhausted budget. Carriage returns become newlines, control characters other than tab are dropped and trailing blanks are
trimmed. **Operator patterns** (`git.sanitize_patterns`, now in the config schema) remove a whole matching line and
are reported as `operator.<n>` (1-based); they must be in the validated subset documented in `safe-pattern.ts` and ADR-0025
(no back-references or look-around, no quantifier on a group holding a quantifier, at most one unbounded quantifier, repeat
counts up to 64, 16 patterns of 200 characters, estimated cost at most 2^26 steps), flags `iu`, matched in windows of 1024
characters under a one-second budget. A `MESSAGE_EMPTY_AFTER_SANITISING` result means the text was empty after the
rules ran.
Fixtures: `src/git/sanitize.test.ts`.

### 12.5 Ref snapshot and violation handling

At LEASED the controller records `git for-each-ref` for the whole repository (all branches, tags and other refs, bounded
in size) as a hash plus the list of ref names and SHAs in a `run_event` payload. At FINALIZING it repeats the listing and
compares: expected differences are the run branch and the publish ref moved by the controller. For the run branch it also
checks that every commit between the base and the head was made by the controller. Any other difference, or a foreign
commit on the run branch, appends `git_policy_violation` (names of refs and SHAs, never messages), the foreign commits are
not reachable from the publish ref, and the tree content is kept as the working-tree changes the post-run checkpoint
records (FRD BR-27). The comparison reads the whole repository, so it also covers writes made through the main
repository's Git directory.

**As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.5, [ADR-0027](../adr/0027-ref-snapshots-and-violation-handling.md); owner text
above unchanged).** Code: `src/git/ref-guard.ts` (`RefGuard`), called by `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts` (snapshot after
`prepare`, before compile and spawn; check after the provider exits, before `assessMutation` and the checkpoint) and by
`src/reconcile/reconcile.ts` (check before the evidence checkpoint after a restart). Choices where this section was
silent:

- **Snapshot payload** (`git_ref_snapshot`, `{v: 1, phase: "leased", ...}`): `digest` (SHA-256 over `<sha> <name>\n`
  for every ref in code-unit order), `ref_count`, `refs` as `[name, sha]` pairs for the first refs by name up to 5000
  refs and 1 MiB of names and SHAs, `truncated` and `tail` (`after`, `count`, `digest` of the refs past the cap, without
  the run's own branch and publish ref), `repo_head` and `worktree_head` (`symref`, `sha`), `run_branch` and
  `publish_ref` (`ref`, `sha`). Listing: `git for-each-ref --format=%(objectname) %(refname)` in the main repository
  through the hardened helper. A repository that cannot be read at LEASED fails the run `TOOL_FAILURE`
  (`GIT_REF_SNAPSHOT_FAILED`) before spawn. `get_run` events and `runs show` print the digest and counts, not the list.
- **Expected differences.** The run branch must still be at the run's `pre` checkpoint (the checkpoint moves it only
  after the check); the worktree `HEAD` must be the symbolic ref to it. The publish ref may be unchanged since LEASED,
  or be a commit with a `git_commit_intent` (not aborted) whose recorded parent chain through such commits reaches the
  meta-session's `base_commit`. Another run's branch may move to one of that run's checkpoint commits or be deleted
  (that run non-terminal, or its session `ARCHIVED`); another session's publish ref may move to one of its intents.
- **Concurrent runs in one repository (T9.11, Delegated).** The checkpoint commit moves a run branch before its `post`
  row exists, so another run's check in that window would report the move as a `switchboard` violation. The lifecycle
  therefore runs each run's check, mutation assessment and checkpoint as one critical section per repository root
  (in-process queue in `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts`; one `serve` per data directory holds the lock). Regression test:
  `src/lifecycle/concurrent-worktrees.test.ts`.
- **Handling.** One `git_policy_violation` event (`v`, `violation_count`, `truncated`, `digest_before`,
  `digest_after`, at most 200 `violations` of `{scope, kind, ref, old, new, action, restore_to?, foreign_commits?
  (at most 50), foreign_commit_count?, detail?}`) is appended before any change; scopes `run_branch`, `worktree_head`,
  `publish_ref`, `repo_head`, `switchboard`, `other`, `unlisted`, `guard`; kinds `created`, `moved`, `deleted`,
  `head_changed`, `unlisted_refs_changed`, `snapshot_missing`, `check_failed`. Then the controller restores, each with
  a compare-and-swap, only the worktree `HEAD` (`symbolic-ref`), the run branch (`update-ref` to `pre`) and the publish
  ref (newest `git_commit` row with a valid chain, else the LEASED value, else deleted); everything else is reported
  only. A `git_ref_check` event (`clean`, `violation` with per-ref restore results, `failed`, `skipped`) closes the
  check; a run with one is not checked again. The run's outcome is unchanged; the terminal transition payload gains
  `git_policy_violation: true` and `runs show` prints `git policy: VIOLATION (n ...)` and `--json` `run.git_policy_violations`.
- **Fail closed.** A check that cannot complete is a `check_failed` violation; a run that reached RUNNING without a
  snapshot is `snapshot_missing` and its own refs are still checked against the ledger.
- **Not covered.** Per-worktree refs (`refs/worktree/`, `refs/bisect/`), objects written without a ref, and a push that
  updates no local ref (BR-27: prevention is the control). Changes made by the operator during the run are flagged too
  (ROADMAP R-47). Verified on Git 2.43.0 only.

### 12.6 Push algorithm

1. Policy `branch`; the BR-31 credential-reach rule holds; the run (or, for an operator command, the meta-session) has a
   publish ref with at least one commit.
2. Compute the refspec from configuration: the publish ref to `<branch_prefix><sess>` on the configured remote. Refuse if
   the target equals the repository default branch or the checked-out branch, is a tag, or would not be a fast-forward
   (never force).
3. Run `git push` from the controller process with that explicit refspec, hooks disabled, using the operator's existing
   Git configuration for the remote. Capture the outcome; redact the output (no URLs with userinfo).
4. In one transaction insert the `git_push` row and event, and mark the pushed `git_commit` rows by recording the remote
   and ref in the event payload (the table itself stays append-only).

**As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.6, [ADR-0028](../adr/0028-controller-push-and-credential-reach.md); owner text
above unchanged).** Code: `src/git/push.ts` (`GitPusher`), `src/git/push-names.ts`, `src/git/credential-reach.ts`,
callers `src/git/channel.ts` (agent tool) and `src/cli/git-push.ts` (operator command `switchboard git push
<sess_...|run_...> [--json]`). Choices where this section was silent:

- **Target.** The local publish ref `refs/heads/switchboard/pub/<sess>` (ADR-0025, Q-42 confirmed) is pushed to
  `refs/heads/<push.branch_prefix><sess>`. The refspec is `<verified head sha>:<target>`: never a ref name as source,
  never a leading `+`. Remote and prefix come from the CURRENT configuration at each push; the policy must be `branch`
  there and, for the agent tool, also in the run's frozen snapshot.
- **Order of checks** (each refusal is `REFUSED_POLICY` with a reason; nothing reaches Git before step 5): (1) the
  meta-session has a project and its primary context is configured (`NO_PROJECT`, `CONTEXT_UNKNOWN`); (2) policy
  `branch` (`POLICY_NONE`); (3) agent only: the BR-31 verdict passed (`CREDENTIAL_REACH`, with the rule's reason in the
  event); (4) no `git_policy_violation` event in any run of the meta-session (`GIT_POLICY_VIOLATION`, Q-44); a valid
  remote name and prefix (`REMOTE_INVALID`, `PREFIX_INVALID`); (5) through the hardened helper: the context path
  (absolute) resolves to the meta-session's repository (`CONTEXT_MISMATCH`), `git check-ref-format` of the target and
  not a run-branch name (`PREFIX_INVALID`), the remote is listed by `git remote` (`REMOTE_UNKNOWN`); (6) the publish ref
  exists and differs from the base commit (`NOTHING_TO_PUSH`); the base is an ancestor of the head and every commit of
  `rev-list <head> --not <base>` (at most 10 000) has a `git_commit` row of this meta-session
  (`PUBLISH_HISTORY_UNVERIFIED`); (7) the target is not the base ref, the remote's `HEAD` branch
  (`refs/remotes/<remote>/HEAD`), the main checkout's branch or any worktree's branch (`TARGET_PROTECTED`); (8) the
  repository's own `local`/`worktree` configuration sets none of `core.sshCommand`, `core.gitProxy`, `core.askPass`,
  `credential.*helper`, `remote.*.receivepack|uploadpack|vcs|mirror`, `url.*.insteadOf|pushInsteadOf`,
  `http.*sslVerify`, `protocol.*` (`REPO_CONFIG_UNSAFE`; key names only); (9) every push URL of the remote
  (`remote get-url --push --all`) is `https` without userinfo, `ssh`/`file` without a password, scp-like or a local path
  (`REMOTE_URL_CREDENTIALS`, `REMOTE_TRANSPORT_NOT_ALLOWED`); the URL stays in memory.
- **The push process** (the only Git call with the operator's configuration): environment allowlist `PATH`, `HOME`,
  `USER`, `LOGNAME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`, `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS`, `HTTPS_PROXY`,
  `https_proxy`, `ALL_PROXY`, `all_proxy`, `NO_PROXY`, `no_proxy`, `SSL_CERT_FILE`, `SSL_CERT_DIR`, plus
  `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, `LC_ALL=C`; `detached` (own session, no terminal), standard input closed, 90 s timeout
  killing the process group; `git -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null -c core.fsmonitor=false -c push.default=nothing -c
  push.followTags=false -c push.gpgSign=false -c push.recurseSubmodules=no -c protocol.allow=never -c
  protocol.https.allow=always -c protocol.ssh.allow=always -c protocol.file.allow=always push --porcelain --no-verify
  --no-follow-tags --no-signed --recurse-submodules=no -- <remote> <sha>:<target>` with `cwd` the main repository.
- **Outcome.** From the `--porcelain` status line of the target: `*`, ` ` or `+` with exit 0 is `ACCEPTED`, `=` is
  `ACCEPTED`/`UP_TO_DATE`, `!` is `REJECTED_REMOTE` with `NON_FAST_FORWARD` (`non-fast-forward`, `fetch first`),
  `REMOTE_HOOK_DECLINED` (`hook declined`) or `REMOTE_REJECTED`. Without a status line, fixed substrings of standard
  error give `ERROR` with `CREDENTIALS_UNAVAILABLE`, `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE`, `REMOTE_TRANSPORT_NOT_ALLOWED`, `TIMEOUT` or
  `GIT_FAILED`. Git's output is then discarded.
- **Record.** `git_push` row plus `git_push` event `{push_id, outcome, reason, requested_by, remote, ref, head_sha,
  commit_count, commits (newest first, at most 500), commits_truncated?, credential_reach?}`. Pushes of one
  meta-session are serialised in the process. Agent tool codes: `PUSH_NOT_ALLOWED`, `NOTHING_TO_PUSH`,
  `REMOTE_REJECTED`, `CREDENTIALS_UNAVAILABLE`, `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE`, `GIT_FAILED`.
- **BR-31** (`evaluateCredentialReach`, from the frozen snapshot, at channel open and at each call): `host` only (a
  `container` snapshot is refused until M10), frozen policy `branch`, no `bypassApproved` or scope `bypass` claim
  without a controller-verified approval, and shell `none` or `allowlist` with an empty list (a `privileged` run by its
  scope's `shell`). No manifest declares credential confinement, so that route is not implemented. *Amended 2026-10-13
  (owner decision 37, Delegated narrow reading; [ADR-0028](../adr/0028-controller-push-and-credential-reach.md)
  "Amended before merge"):* the caller passes the controller-held fact `approvedBypass` (`bypassApprovedFor`: an
  APPROVED, hash-matching approval row whose scope sets `bypass`, the same verdict as the argv audit gate); when true
  and policy is `branch` on a `host` environment the verdict is `ok` with the reason `bypass_exempt`, whatever the
  shell. The reason is recorded in `git_channel_opened.credential_reach`, `git_push.credential_reach` and the
  `git_push` row `detail` (`<REASON>; credential_reach=bypass_exempt`). Nothing else in §12.6 changes. This is the one
  place a bypass run's agent can reach push credentials, by the owner's choice (R-51).
- **Operator command.** Same algorithm without BR-31; takes the data-directory lock and refuses (`LOCKED`) while `serve`
  holds it (ADR-0005, like `gc --apply`); exit 0 when the remote holds the head.
- **Ref guard.** `refs/remotes/<remote>/<branch>` created or moved to a SHA that an ACCEPTED `git_push` row pushed to
  `refs/heads/<branch>` on `<remote>` is an expected difference (§12.5).
- **Not tested.** `https` and `ssh` remotes, credential helpers, SSH agents and proxies (no network in tests); Git
  versions other than 2.43.0.

### 12.7 Provenance and audit

`git_commit` rows are the audit trail (FR-GIT-07). `runs show <id>` joins them to the run's provider, model and adapter
version and prints each commit's SHA, origin (`commit_tool` or `finalize_only`), push state and sanitiser counts.
Re-linking after an operator rewrite: match first on `commit_sha`, then `tree_sha`, then `patch_id` (best effort; Git's
`patch-id` command; to be verified against the pinned Git version). Records are never edited and the commit objects are
never annotated (notes were rejected, ADR-0014).

*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.6):* `runs show <run>` (`src/cli/runs.ts`) adds `run.commits`: per `git_commit` row
of the run `sha`, `tree_sha`, `patch_id`, `origin`, `publish_ref`, `project_id`, `context_id`, `meta_session_id`,
`run_id`, `provider`, `harness`, `model`, `adapter_version_id`, `adapter_version` (the `adapter_version.binary_version`),
`tool_event_id`, `sanitizer` (rule ids and counts), `created_at` and `pushed_to` (remote name, ref, push id and time of
every ACCEPTED `git_push` event of the meta-session whose commit list holds the SHA); and `run.pushes`: the `git_push`
rows whose event is on this run (`id`, `outcome`, `reason`, `remote`, `ref`, `head_sha`, `requested_by`, `run_id`,
`created_at`). Text and `--json`; never the message or a URL. A re-link after an operator rewrite is not implemented.
*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.7):* each commit also shows `via` (`tool` or `finalize`, from `origin`; text
`<sha>  finalize_only  via finalize`), and `run.finalize_commit` (`outcome`, `reason` or `commit_sha`) comes from the
run's `git_finalize_commit` event (text line `finalize: ...`).

**Inner-agent provenance (M11, Proposed).** `git_commit` gains `message_source` (`agent` or `inner`, default `agent`) and
`inner_call_id` (the `inner_call` row of the accepted draft; NULL for an agent message). `runs show` prints both. The
message itself is never annotated or stored; the PR draft's provenance is its `pr_draft` artifact ID on the `inner_call`
run event.

*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T11.4):* migration `0007_git_commit_message_source`
(`src/ledger/migrations/0007_git_commit_message_source.ts`; ledger schema version 7) adds `message_source TEXT NOT NULL
DEFAULT 'agent' CHECK (message_source IN ('agent','inner'))` and `inner_call_id TEXT REFERENCES inner_call(id) CHECK
(inner_call_id IS NULL OR message_source = 'inner')`; existing rows read `agent`/NULL and the append-only guards are
unchanged. `inner_call_id` is NULL with `inner` only when the call row could not be written. The `git_commit_intent`
record and the `git_commit` event payload gain `message_source: "inner"` and `inner_call_id` only for a drafted
message, so an agent commit's records are unchanged. `runs show` adds `message_source` and `inner_call_id` (JSON) and a
`message:` line (text).

## 13. Inner agent

Implements FR-INN-01..17 and [ADR-0016](../adr/0016-inner-agent.md) for M11 (COMP-INN, trust boundary TB-10). Task
catalogue and recommendations: [PRD §3.1](../requirements/PRD.md#31-inner-agent-task-catalogue). Everything here is
**Proposed** unless an SRS ID fixes it; no model name, port or provider flag is stated as fact, and every number is a
*(proposed target)*. An inner call is a stateless single-turn text completion with no tools (FR-INN-02). No inner output
is an input to COMP-POL, profile compilation, eligibility, approvals, ceilings, push policy, the sanitiser's rules,
redaction, failure classification or any run status (FR-INN-06); structure and the import-boundary test
([TDD §3.12](TDD.md#312-inner-agent)) enforce it, not prompting.

### 13.1 Configuration shape

Part of the config schema ([TDD §3.1](TDD.md#31-configuration)). With no `inner_agent` section, or `enabled: false`, every
task uses its fallback and nothing else changes (FR-INN-01). There is no default backend, model or task.

```jsonc
{ "inner_agent": {
    "enabled": true,                                   // master switch; default false
    "backend": {
      "kind": "http",                                  // or "cli" (FR-INN-04; Ineligible until verified, §13.2)
      "base_url": "http://127.0.0.1:<port>",           // http only; no userinfo, no query; or a Unix socket path form (§13.2)
      "model": "<model>",                              // operator-chosen placeholder; never defaulted
      "timeout_ms": 15000,                             // hard bound per task call, both attempts together (proposed target)
      "max_concurrency": 2,                            // per-backend semaphore (proposed target)
      "allow_remote": false,                           // true is required to use a backend classified remote (FR-INN-16)
      "shares_subscription_with": null                 // optional provider id; see FR-INN-15
      // cli kind instead of base_url: "provider": "opencode"   (flags and argv are adapter-owned, not configurable here)
    },
    "tasks": {
      "commit_message":   { "enabled": true,  "mode": "draft_if_missing",   // draft_if_missing | always | never
                            "allow_source_content": false, "input_budget_bytes": 24000, "max_output_tokens": 400 },
      "pr_draft":         { "enabled": false, "allow_source_content": false, "input_budget_bytes": 48000, "max_output_tokens": 1200 },
      "route_features":   { "enabled": false, "input_budget_bytes": 8000, "max_output_tokens": 100, "timeout_ms": 5000 },
      "handoff_condense": { "enabled": false, "allow_source_content": false, "input_budget_bytes": 32000, "max_output_tokens": 800 },
      "log_summary":      { "enabled": false, "allow_source_content": false, "input_budget_bytes": 32000, "max_output_tokens": 400 },
      "run_title":        { "enabled": false, "input_budget_bytes": 2000, "max_output_tokens": 80 }
    },
    "breaker":    { "failure_threshold": 3, "open_ms": 60000 },                          // proposed targets
    "suspension": { "window_calls": 50, "min_calls": 20, "rejection_threshold": 0.4 }    // proposed targets (FR-INN-09)
  } }
```

Rules (Ajv at startup; an unknown key or type error aborts startup, NFR-SEC-06):

- **No credential fields.** There is no key, token, header, password or environment-variable field. `base_url` is
  rejected if it carries userinfo or a query string; a value that looks like a credential is rejected at load (T11.1). A
  keyed hosted service is reached through an operator-run local gateway that holds the key, or through the `cli` kind,
  which keeps its own login (FR-INN-03, ADR-0003).
- `mode` is valid only on `commit_message`. `allow_source_content` is valid only on tasks that send repository content
  (`commit_message`, `pr_draft`, `handoff_condense`, `log_summary`; §13.5) and defaults to `false`. A task `timeout_ms`
  may only be lower than the backend's. `input_budget_bytes` has a floor of 1024 so the fixed header always fits.
- `shares_subscription_with` must name a configured provider id or be `null` (FR-INN-15).
- **Per-project and per-context setting.** The project object and each context object of [§11.1](#111-configuration-shape)
  gain an optional `"inner_agent": "off" | "local_only" | "inherit"` (default `inherit`). There is deliberately no `on`
  value: a project can only restrict what the installation-level configuration allows. The effective value is the most
  restrictive of project and context (`off` over `local_only` over `inherit`). Runs started by `workspace_path`, with no
  project, use `inherit`.
- Tasks are enabled individually and none is on by default (FR-INN-05). A task whose master switch, task switch, mode,
  privacy gate or suspension forbids the call uses its fallback (§13.7).

### 13.2 Backend port

```ts
type InnerTask = 'commit_message' | 'pr_draft' | 'route_features' | 'handoff_condense' | 'log_summary' | 'run_title';
type Locality = 'local' | 'remote';

interface InnerRequest {
  task: InnerTask;
  template_version: string;            // e.g. "commit_message.v1"; part of the task contract, bumped on any prompt change
  system: string;                      // fixed template text owned by the task contract, never built from input
  user: string;                        // redacted, budget-truncated input (§13.4 steps 2-3)
  max_output_tokens: number;
  response_schema: object;             // JSON Schema of the expected output; a hint to the backend, never trusted (§13.4)
}

interface InnerResult {
  text: string;                        // raw completion text; untrusted data (FR-INN-07)
  usage?: { in: number | null; out: number | null };   // as reported by the backend; null/absent = unknown, never estimated
  model_id: string;                    // as reported by the backend, else the configured model string
}

interface InnerBackend {
  readonly kind: 'http' | 'cli';
  resolveLocality(signal: AbortSignal): Promise<Locality>;   // at connect time, every connection (rule below)
  complete(req: InnerRequest, signal: AbortSignal): Promise<InnerResult>;   // rejects on transport/exit failure
  probe(signal: AbortSignal): Promise<{ reachable: boolean; canary_ok: boolean; eligible: boolean; detail: string }>;
}
```

The port carries one request and one answer. There is no tool, file, session, history or streaming parameter, so a
backend cannot be asked for more than a single completion (FR-INN-02). How `InnerRequest` maps onto a chat-completion
request body, whether `response_schema` is passed as a structured-output constraint, and how usage is read are **Proposed**
details of the `http` implementation, fixed in T11.3 against the endpoint the operator runs; the controller validates the
output the same way whether or not the backend honoured the schema.

**Kinds.**

- `http`: a chat-completion endpoint the operator runs or reaches (FR-INN-03). Model and endpoint come only from the
  configuration. No redirects are followed and no proxy environment variable is read (Proposed).
- `cli`: an installed provider CLI run once, headless, with an operator-chosen model, the first being `opencode run`
  (FR-INN-04, FR-ADP-08). **Proposed; flags unverified (Q-26, T11.0).** The run uses an empty temporary directory, a
  minimal environment (FR-EXE-07), no repository config, and only argv that passes the adapter argv audit (no bypass flag;
  [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md)). The way the prompt is delivered (argv, stdin or file) and the output read are fixed by
  the T11.0 spike, not guessed here.

**Ineligible rule (cli).** The `cli` backend is `eligible` only if (a) the installed version is inside a compliance
manifest range (FR-ADP-03) and (b) the documented controls that offer the model no tools, no file and no shell access have
been shown to apply for that version (T11.0). Otherwise `probe` returns `eligible: false`, the backend is reported
`Ineligible` in `doctor`, no process is started, and every task uses its fallback. Nothing downgrades this to "run it
anyway".

**Locality classification** (FR-INN-16). `local` means the address the controller actually connects to is a loopback
address (`127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`) or a Unix domain socket on the host; everything else is `remote`.

- Resolved **at connect time, not only at config load**: for `http`, the hostname is resolved when the connection is made,
  the controller connects to that resolved address (so a second lookup cannot differ), and the peer address of the
  established socket is checked. A hostname resolving to several addresses is `local` only if every address is loopback.
  A name that was loopback at load but resolves elsewhere now is `remote` for that call; if `allow_remote` is false the
  call is denied (§13.5). A failure to resolve is `remote` (fail closed).
- The `cli` kind is always `remote` (Proposed): the controller cannot see where the installed CLI sends the prompt.
- The classification used for each call is stored in `inner_call.locality` and shown by `doctor`.

*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T11.3; `src/inner/http-backend.ts`):* the points §13.2 left to T11.3, all **Proposed**
and marked **Assumption**: (1) request: `POST <base_url>/v1/chat/completions` (`<base_url>/chat/completions` when the
base path already ends in `/v1`), JSON body `{ model, messages: [{role: "system"}, {role: "user"}], max_tokens,
temperature: 0, stream: false }`; `response_schema` is not sent, because structured-output parameters differ between
servers and the pipeline validates the output either way; (2) answer: `choices[0].message.content` must be a string,
usage is `usage.prompt_tokens` and `usage.completion_tokens` when non-negative integers (else unknown), the model id is
the response's `model` or the configured one; (3) headers are fixed and none comes from configuration or the
environment; the request asks for `identity` encoding and a compressed answer is a failure; (4) any 3xx is a failure and
is not followed, no proxy variable is read, and each call uses its own connection; (5) the response may not exceed
`max_output_tokens * 16` bytes plus 16 KiB (declared `content-length` is checked first); (6) locality: `resolveLocality`
resolves the host once, and the address is pinned to the call's `AbortSignal` so `complete` connects to that exact
address; the connected socket's peer is re-checked and a call gated `local` whose peer is not loopback is aborted before
the body is written. `private` (RFC 1918, link-local, CGNAT, unique-local) is `remote`, as FR-INN-16 says "anything
else"; (7) `probe` sends only a fixed canary, returns code-only `detail`, and does not contact an endpoint classified
`remote` when `allow_remote` is false. The Unix-socket form of `base_url` mentioned in §13.1 remains undefined and
unimplemented.

### 13.3 Data

Added by the M11 forward migration ([§1](#1-data-model)). Same conventions as §1: ULID text IDs with a prefix (`inc_`,
Proposed), UTC millisecond timestamps, `STRICT`, no prompt or response body in any column (FR-INN-16).

```sql
CREATE TABLE inner_call (               -- append-only (FR-INN-15, FR-INN-09); never usage_observation, never capacity
  id              TEXT PRIMARY KEY CHECK (id LIKE 'inc\_%' ESCAPE '\'),
  task            TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (task IN ('commit_message','pr_draft','route_features','handoff_condense',
                    'log_summary','run_title')),
  backend_kind    TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (backend_kind IN ('http','cli')),
  model_id        TEXT NOT NULL,        -- reported by the backend, else the configured model string
  locality        TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (locality IN ('local','remote')),
  template_version TEXT NOT NULL,
  outcome         TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (outcome IN ('accepted','rejected_validator','rejected_sanitiser',
                    'fallback_unavailable','fallback_suspended','fallback_disabled')),
  reason          TEXT,                 -- short code only (e.g. REMOTE_NOT_ALLOWED, CIRCUIT_OPEN, TIMEOUT); no free text
  attempts        INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (attempts BETWEEN 0 AND 2),   -- 0 when no call was made; 2 after a re-ask
  latency_ms      INTEGER NOT NULL,     -- wall time of the whole pipeline attempt, 0 when no call was made
  tokens_in       INTEGER,              -- as reported; NULL = unknown (never estimated)
  tokens_out      INTEGER,
  input_bytes     INTEGER NOT NULL,     -- size of the redacted, budget-truncated user text actually sent
  output_bytes    INTEGER NOT NULL,     -- size of the raw completion received (0 if none)
  input_sha256    TEXT NOT NULL,        -- hash of that sent text; lets a test or operator match without storing it
  run_id          TEXT REFERENCES run(id),                 -- NULL for calls outside a run (inner test, doctor canary)
  meta_session_id TEXT REFERENCES meta_session(id),
  created_at      TEXT NOT NULL
) STRICT;
CREATE INDEX inner_call_task_time ON inner_call(task, created_at);
CREATE INDEX inner_call_run ON inner_call(run_id);

CREATE TABLE inner_task_state (         -- mutable current state, one row per task (FR-INN-09)
  task            TEXT PRIMARY KEY CHECK (task IN ('commit_message','pr_draft','route_features','handoff_condense',
                    'log_summary','run_title')),
  suspended       INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (suspended IN (0,1)),
  suspended_at    TEXT,
  suspended_basis_json TEXT CHECK (suspended_basis_json IS NULL OR json_valid(suspended_basis_json)), -- window, rejected, total, threshold
  resumed_at      TEXT,                 -- window counting restarts after this instant
  version         INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0
) STRICT;
```

**T11.1 note (Delegated).** `inner_call` ships alone in migration 0005 (`src/ledger/migrations/0005_inner_call.ts`).
`inner_task_state` arrives with T11.6, and the `git_commit` columns, the `usage_observation.source` widening and the
`pr_draft` artifact kind with T11.4, T11.5 and T11.8, each as its own forward migration, because a released migration is
immutable (ADR-0005).

`inner_call` gets the same `BEFORE UPDATE`/`DELETE` guards as the other append-only tables. The outcomes mean: `accepted`
(the output passed every check and was used); `rejected_validator` (not valid after the permitted re-ask);
`rejected_sanitiser` (valid, but the sanitiser left nothing usable); `fallback_unavailable` (the call could not be made
or completed: unreachable, timeout, error, open circuit, no free slot, `Ineligible`); `fallback_suspended` (the task is
suspended, §13.4 step 11); `fallback_disabled` (the task is enabled but a gate forbade the call: locality, `allow_remote`,
`allow_source_content`, project setting, or the backend switched off). A task that is simply not enabled, or a
`commit_message` call skipped because the agent supplied a message in `draft_if_missing`, writes no row.

The accepted result lives where it is used, not in `inner_call`: a commit message in the commit, a PR draft and condensed
text in artifacts, route features in `route_decision.features_json`, a run title in a `run_event` payload (§13.6). The
`usage_observation` exception for `shares_subscription_with` is in §13.4 step 13.

**T11.6 note (Delegated).** `inner_task_state` ships as migration 0008
(`src/ledger/migrations/0008_inner_task_state.ts`, the DDL above unchanged). A row exists only once a task has been
suspended; no row reads as not suspended. A suspension sets `suspended = 1`, `suspended_at` and `suspended_basis_json`
(`window_calls`, `rejected`, `total`, `threshold`) and increments `version`. A re-enable sets `suspended = 0`, clears
`suspended_at` and the basis, sets `resumed_at` and increments `version`; the suspension evidence then survives only in
the `inner_task_suspended` run event (when the suspending call was tied to a run). The window counts calls with `created_at`
strictly after `resumed_at`, so a call recorded in the same millisecond as the resume is not counted (Assumption: the
safe side; counting an older call would suspend again at once). The window is evaluated by `Ledger.recordInnerCall` in
the transaction that inserts the row, and the gate reads `inner_task_state` on every call, so the state is shared by every
process on the ledger. A suspended call records `fallback_suspended` with reason `TASK_SUSPENDED`, `attempts` 0 and
locality `remote` (the unresolved default, as for `PROJECT_OFF`). An unreadable state fails closed to the fallback
(`fallback_unavailable`, `STATE_UNREADABLE`). The re-enable is `InnerAgentService.resumeTask` (the `inner resume`
command of FR-INN-17, T11.7) with no config edit or restart needed.

**T11.4 note (Delegated).** The two `git_commit` columns ship as migration 0007
(`src/ledger/migrations/0007_git_commit_message_source.ts`; [§12.7](#127-provenance-and-audit)). An `inner_call` row
with outcome `accepted` exists for every draft handed to the publisher; when the commit then fails after the draft (a
Git failure, a lineage or signing refusal, or a second lost compare-and-swap), that row stays and no `git_commit` row
points to it.

**T11.5 note (Delegated).** The `usage_observation.source` widening ships as migration 0006
(`src/ledger/migrations/0006_usage_inner_agent.ts`, a table rebuild). The step 13 observation is written only when a
backend request was made (`attempts >= 1`); `harness` is the provider id, `model` the call's `model_id`, and
`metrics_json` holds `input_tokens` and `output_tokens` when reported (key names are an Assumption), else `{}`.

### 13.4 Call pipeline

One pipeline serves every task; the task contract (§13.6) supplies the input builder, template, schema, validator and
fallback. Redaction is the same component and rules as telemetry (FR-TEL-03, ADR-0012), not a second implementation.

1. **Gate.** If `enabled` is false or the task is not enabled, return the fallback with no row. If the task is suspended
   (`inner_task_state.suspended`), record `fallback_suspended` and return the fallback. Otherwise evaluate the privacy
   gates of §13.5; a denial records `fallback_disabled` with the reason code and returns the fallback. No bytes of task
   input are sent before this point; only name resolution for `resolveLocality` may occur.
2. **Build and redact.** The task's input builder takes typed controller-owned fields (never a run object, a transcript
   or a request body) and produces the text. Then, over every string in it, in this order: (a) the telemetry redactor
   (credential-like values, home paths, environment values), (b) the identifier scrub: the patterns of §12.4 rules
   `id.controller` and `id.native-session` and the configured provider names, so the prompt carries none of them
   (FR-INN-10), (c) control-character stripping. The redactors only remove or mask; nothing the model returns is ever fed
   back into them (FR-INN-06, FR-INN-18).
3. **Budget.** If the text exceeds `input_budget_bytes`, truncate deterministically, never in the middle of a UTF-8
   sequence, and insert a marker line `[truncated: <n> bytes omitted]`. Diffs: keep the file list and per-file stat header
   whole, then whole hunks in path order until the budget is used. Logs and long text: keep a head of 25% and a tail of
   75% of the budget (proposed target split). The marker and the fixed template are always kept. Truncation is recorded
   only through `input_bytes`.
4. **Hash.** `input_sha256` is computed over the text that will be sent.
5. **Slot.** Acquire the per-backend semaphore (`max_concurrency`) waiting no longer than the remaining per-call bound.
   No slot in time: `fallback_unavailable` (`BUSY`).
6. **Breaker.** `closed`: proceed. `open`: do not call; `fallback_unavailable` (`CIRCUIT_OPEN`). `half_open`: exactly one
   call at a time goes through as the probe; others see `open`. Transitions:
   - `closed` to `open` after `breaker.failure_threshold` consecutive transport-level failures (connect error, timeout,
     non-success response, unreadable response envelope, process failure for `cli`) across all tasks on the backend;
   - `open` to `half_open` after `breaker.open_ms`;
   - `half_open` to `closed` on a call that returns a transport-level success, to `open` on a failure.
   A validator or sanitiser rejection is a quality outcome, not a transport failure: it does not move the breaker. Breaker
   state is held in memory and starts `closed` at process start (Assumption; `doctor` re-probes).
7. **Call.** The deadline is `timeout_ms` (task value if lower) for the whole pipeline including a re-ask; the
   `AbortSignal` cancels the request or kills the `cli` process group at the deadline. The caller is never blocked longer.
8. **Decode and strip.** The raw text must be valid UTF-8 (otherwise invalid), is capped at the task's output byte limit
   (longer is invalid, not truncated), stripped of control characters other than newline and tab, and, for JSON tasks,
   parsed as a single JSON value with nothing else around it.
9. **Validate.** Ajv against the task's output schema (`additionalProperties: false`), then the task's validator rules
   (§13.6). On failure, **one re-ask** if time remains: the same request plus a fixed error class from a closed list
   (`not_json`, `schema`, `too_long`, `empty`, `rule`), never the rejected text, so hostile output is not echoed back. A
   second failure is `rejected_validator`.
10. **Sanitiser (commit_message and pr_draft only).** Run the [§12.4](#124-message-sanitisation) sanitiser unchanged over
    the text. Empty or unusable afterwards is `rejected_sanitiser`, with no re-ask. Sanitiser counts go to
    `git_commit.sanitizer_json` as for any message.
11. **Quality window and suspension.** Over the task's last `suspension.window_calls` calls with outcome `accepted`,
    `rejected_validator` or `rejected_sanitiser` since `inner_task_state.resumed_at` (`fallback_*` outcomes are not quality
    evidence and are not counted), if at least `suspension.min_calls` exist and the rejected share exceeds
    `suspension.rejection_threshold`, set `suspended = 1` in one transaction. The task then returns `fallback_suspended`
    until the operator re-enables it (FR-INN-09). The re-enable command is `inner resume <task>`, named in FR-INN-17
    (T11.7). Suspension never reverses a result already accepted.
12. **Accept or fall back.** Accepted: return the validated value to the caller, which performs every action that follows
    (FR-INN-02). Any rejection or failure: return the task's deterministic fallback (§13.7). In no case does the caller's
    run change state because of the result (FR-INN-08).
13. **Record.** In one ledger transaction: the `inner_call` row; for a call tied to a run, one `run_event` of kind
    `inner_call` (plus `inner_degraded` or `inner_task_suspended` when a transition happened, §13.8); and, only when
    `shares_subscription_with` names a provider, one `usage_observation` for that provider with `source = 'inner_agent'`
    and the reported token counts in `metrics_json` (empty when unknown). `provider_health`, the capacity estimator and
    `capacity_score` are not touched (FR-INN-15). Bodies are never written.

Every step is wrapped so that an exception inside the pipeline becomes `fallback_unavailable`; the pipeline has no code
path that throws into the run supervisor.

### 13.5 Privacy gates

Evaluated in step 1 of §13.4, before any task data leaves the process. "Source content" is repository-derived text:
diffs and file lists with hunks (`commit_message`, `pr_draft`), log or verification output excerpts (`log_summary`),
diff excerpts and decision text carried into a handoff (`handoff_condense`). `route_features` and `run_title` send the
request's objective text and counts only, which is not repository content; whether a remote backend should see an
objective without a second opt-in is an open point (Assumption: `allow_remote` alone is enough).

| # | Effective project setting | Backend locality | `allow_remote` | Task sends source content | Task `allow_source_content` | Result | Reason code |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | `off` | any | any | any | any | Denied | `PROJECT_OFF` |
| 2 | `local_only` | remote | any | any | any | Denied | `PROJECT_LOCAL_ONLY` |
| 3 | `local_only` or `inherit` | local | any | any | any | Allowed | n/a |
| 4 | `inherit` | remote | false | any | any | Denied | `REMOTE_NOT_ALLOWED` |
| 5 | `inherit` | remote | true | no | any | Allowed | n/a |
| 6 | `inherit` | remote | true | yes | false | Denied | `SOURCE_CONTENT_NOT_ALLOWED` |
| 7 | `inherit` | remote | true | yes | true | Allowed | n/a |

A denial is `fallback_disabled`. Further rules: `cli` is `remote` (§13.2), so rows 4 to 7 apply to it; an unresolved
locality is `remote`; an `Ineligible` backend is `fallback_unavailable` (`INELIGIBLE`), not a privacy result.
`inner test <task>` and the `doctor` canary use a built-in fixture with no repository data, so they are subject to
rows 4 and 5 only. In every allowed case redaction (§13.4 step 2) still runs, and bodies are still not logged (FR-INN-16).
`allow_source_content` is meaningless for a `local` backend (row 3) and is ignored there.

### 13.6 Task contracts

Each task has a versioned template (`<task>.v1`, Proposed), an input budget, an output schema, a validator and a
fallback (FR-INN-05). All output schemas use `additionalProperties: false`. All input builders **exclude**: controller,
session and run IDs, native provider session IDs, provider, adapter and model names, internal run-branch and publish-ref
names (they contain session IDs), absolute home paths, credentials, and the raw agent transcript. Paths are
worktree-relative. Output length limits are proposed targets.

| Task (`FR`) | Input fields | Output schema | Validator rules beyond the schema | Fallback |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `commit_message` (FR-INN-10) | Paths being committed; unified diff of exactly those paths; up to 5 recent subject lines of the publish ref (style hint); nothing else. The agent's own message is not sent, and neither is the objective | `{ "subject": string 1..72, "body"?: string 0..2000 }` | Subject is one line, no leading or trailing space, no control characters, no code fence; body has no `Co-authored-by`-style key line (the sanitiser also removes them); result = `subject` plus blank line plus `body` | No message: tool error `MESSAGE_REQUIRED` (§12.1); never an invented message |
| `pr_draft` (FR-INN-11) | Subjects of the controller-made commits on the publish ref since its base; changed file list with line counts; diff excerpt within budget; controller-computed verification facts (command label, pass or fail) if any | `{ "title": string 1..100, "body": string 1..8000 }` | Single-line title; markdown body; if the verification facts hold no pass, or hold a fail, the draft is rejected when it matches a success claim (for example "all tests pass"; pattern list Proposed); no URLs added (Proposed) | No `pr_draft` artifact; `get_artifacts` returns none and the orchestrator writes its own. Stored as artifact kind `pr_draft`, `application/json`, referenced by the `inner_call` run event |
| `route_features` (FR-INN-12) | Objective text (truncated); counts: constraints, verification commands, repository file count and size band, extension histogram; no trust profile, no `provider_preference`, no provider names | `{ "context_volume": "small"\|"medium"\|"large", "autonomy": "low"\|"medium"\|"high", "reasoning_depth": "low"\|"medium"\|"high", "task_kind": "bug_fix"\|"feature"\|"refactor"\|"test"\|"docs"\|"investigation"\|"other" }` | Enumerations only, no free text, all four keys present; stage-2 scoring input only, never read by eligibility or by `require_provider` handling | The heuristic classifier (FR-RTE-04); if it too is unavailable, the neutral value `medium` for each level and `other` |
| `handoff_condense` (FR-INN-13) | Labelled controller-owned text fields of the bundle (goal, constraints, decisions, failed-attempt notes, open tasks) and bounded diff excerpts; statuses, exit codes and counts are not sent | `{ "fields": { "<name>": string 1..600 } }`, names limited to the bundle's prose fields | Every key is a requested field; each value is no longer than its source field; every digit sequence in a value occurs in the source (no invented counts or codes); the bundle stores `{ text, source: "inner", model_id, template_version, raw_artifact_id, raw_sha256 }` per condensed field | Deterministic truncation of the raw text with a truncation marker; structured bundle fields are unchanged either way |
| `log_summary` (FR-INN-13) | Head and tail excerpt of the verification output or log artifact; controller facts as labelled lines (command label, exit code, counts) given as facts to restate, not infer | `{ "summary": string 1..600, "notable"?: string[] (max 5, each 1..160) }` | Same digit-in-source rule; no sentence may be the only carrier of a status: the envelope's `status`, exit codes and counts come from records, not from this text; stored with source label and raw artifact ID plus hash | Deterministic head and tail excerpt with line and byte counts, labelled `source: heuristic` |
| `run_title` (FR-INN-14, Could) | First N characters of the objective (within budget) | `{ "title": string 1..80, "summary"?: string 1..200 }` | Single line each, no control characters, no markdown, no URLs; treated as data in every listing | First line of the objective truncated to 80 characters. Accepted values go in a `run_event` payload of kind `inner_call` (Proposed; no run-table column) |

The `commit_message` task modes (FR-INN-10): `draft_if_missing` draws on the inner agent only when the agent supplied no
message; `always` requests a draft in every commit and uses it when valid, otherwise the agent's message if present;
`never` disables the task for commits and keeps `message` required. A route feature is stored in
`route_decision.features_json` as `{ "value": ..., "source": "heuristic" | "inner", "model_id"?: string,
"template_version"?: string }` per feature; replay reads the stored value and never calls the model (FR-RTE-03,
FR-INN-12). Handoff and log text always carry a visible source label so a reader can tell inner prose from controller
facts.

*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T11.4; table above unchanged), `commit_message`:* template `commit_message.v1`
(`src/inner/commit-message.ts`), a fixed system text that names no product, provider, tool or identifier. Points this
section left open, each an **Assumption**:

- **Body trailer lines are removed, not rejected.** The validator column says "body has no `Co-authored-by`-style key
  line (the sanitiser also removes them)". Rejecting would make a backend that always adds trailers produce no commit,
  against the T11.4 Done-when ("yields a clean commit") and the M11 completion criterion in the ROADMAP, which owns
  completion criteria. So such lines in the body are left to the §12.4 sanitiser, which removes them and counts them;
  the subject, which must survive the sanitiser, is rejected when it is a trailer-shaped key line (`<key>-by:` or
  `<key>-with:`). The other subject rules are as in the table; body control characters other than newline and tab are
  refused.
- **Input.** Under the fixed headings `Changed paths (added<TAB>deleted<TAB>path):` (`git diff-tree --numstat`),
  `Recent subjects (style hint):` and `Diff:`. "Recent subjects of the publish ref" are those of the commit's parent,
  which is the publish ref head, or the session's base commit before the first commit.
- **Budget.** The diff-aware truncation of §13.4 step 3 keeps the part before `Diff:` whole, then whole hunks (each
  with its file header) in path order, then the marker; if the header alone does not fit, head-and-tail over the
  header with no hunk. The builder pre-caps the raw diff the same way at four times the budget so the redactor never
  scans an unbounded text.
- **Scrubbed names.** Besides the configured provider ids, the prompt scrub covers `PROMPT_PROVIDER_NAMES` (`claude`,
  `anthropic`, `codex`, `openai`, `gemini`, `antigravity`, `cursor`, `grok`, `kimi`, `opencode`), because a config id
  such as `claude-code` would miss the bare product name; a diff mentioning one of them reaches the model with
  `[provider]` instead.
- **Output cap.** The raw completion may be at most `4 * (72 + 2000) + 1024` bytes.

*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T11.8; table above unchanged), `pr_draft`:* template `pr_draft.v1`
(`src/inner/pr-draft.ts`), a fixed system text that names no product, provider, tool or identifier. Each point is an
**Assumption** unless the table says it:

- **Input.** Under the fixed headings `Commit subjects (oldest first):`, `Changed paths (added<TAB>deleted<TAB>path):`,
  `Verification facts (recorded by the system):` and `Diff:`. The commits are the run's `git_commit` rows; the diff runs
  from the parent of the first to the last of them. Facts come from the run's `verification_command` events, labelled
  `check <n>` because the events hold no command label. Budget and truncation are those of `commit_message`.
- **Sanitiser.** Title and body each pass the §12.4 sanitiser (operator patterns and the run's native session IDs
  included); the SANITISED draft is stored. Trailer and "generated by" lines in the body are removed, not rejected (as for
  commit messages). The "no URLs added" rule is judged on the text the sanitiser leaves.
- **Persistence.** The pipeline's optional `persist` hook stores the accepted draft before the call is recorded, so the
  `inner_call` run event carries the `pr_draft` artifact ID; a storage failure is `fallback_unavailable`
  (`PERSIST_FAILED`) and no draft is handed out.
- **Trigger.** No tool or CLI command starts a draft yet; the entry point is `createPrDraft` (T11.7 adds `inner` commands).
  Retrieval is `get_artifacts` with kind `pr_draft` (§3).
- **Sanitiser in step 10.** The pipeline uses the publisher's sanitiser only to decide `rejected_sanitiser`; it hands
  the validated, unsanitised text back, and the publisher sanitises it once more as the commit's message, so its rule
  counts reach `sanitizer_json`.

*As built (2026-10-14, Delegated, T11.9; table above unchanged), `log_summary`:* template `log_summary.v1`
(`src/inner/log-summary.ts`), a fixed system text that names no product, provider, tool or identifier. Each point is
an **Assumption** unless the table says it:

- **Source.** The run's `test_report` artifact (the raw artifact, FR-EXE-08), parsed into the controller's records:
  per command its index, outcome, exit code, stdout and stderr. The input holds, under the fixed headings `Recorded
  facts (restate, do not infer):` (`check <n>: outcome ..., exit code ...` and a counts line) and `Output excerpt:`,
  those facts and the per-check output. Argv, the objective and every identifier are excluded. One summary covers the
  whole run.
- **Budget.** The facts stay whole; the excerpt takes the head 25% and tail 75% of the rest (§13.4 step 3), pre-capped
  at four times the budget before redaction.
- **Validator.** Beyond the schema: single-line values, no control character, no URL, every whole digit run must occur
  as a whole digit run in the text the model was given (stricter than "occurs in the source", so `17` is not matched
  by `1700`), and a success claim (the `pr_draft` pattern list, Proposed) is refused unless the records hold at least
  one check and every check passed. There is no sanitiser step (§13.4 step 10 is for commit and PR text); the stored
  text is passed through the artifact redactor.
- **Fallback and storage.** When the task is enabled and the model produces no accepted summary (any outcome in
  §13.7), the shown text is the deterministic excerpt: the first 8 and last 12 lines of the combined output with an
  omitted-lines marker, counts of lines and bytes, at most 1,500 bytes, controls stripped, `source: heuristic`. With
  the task not enabled nothing is produced (§13.7 row 1). Either result is stored as a `log_summary` artifact
  (`application/json`, kind already in the schema) and recorded as one `log_summary` run event.
- **Envelope.** The `log_summary` run event (`src/lifecycle/summary-event.ts`) holds `source`, `text`, `notable`, the
  raw artifact ID and SHA-256, the summary artifact ID and `inner_call_id`. It carries text, an exception to "no text"
  in the table of §13.8 (as for `run_title`), because the envelope builder is synchronous and reads the ledger only.
  The envelope shows it as `result.verification_summary { source, label, text, notable?, raw_artifact { id, sha256 },
  summary_artifact_id? }`; the label is derived from `source` by the builder, never from the payload. `status`,
  `failure_code`, `verification` (outcomes, exit codes) and every count come from the run row and the terminal
  transition, not from this field. The envelope builder reads the event by constant and shape and does not import
  `src/inner/` (the import-boundary test is unchanged).
- **Trigger.** No run path calls `summariseVerification` automatically (the lifecycle may not import the service); the
  entry point is the service function, like `createPrDraft`.

*As built (2026-10-15, Delegated, T11.10; table above unchanged), `handoff_condense`:* template `handoff_condense.v1`
(`src/inner/handoff-condense.ts`), a fixed system text that names no product, provider, tool or identifier. Each point
is an **Assumption** unless the table says it:

- **Beside the view, not inside it.** The table says the bundle stores each condensed field. The view is a pure function
  of `(as_of, bounds, builder_version)` (FR-SES-05, ADR-0038) and model output is not regenerable, so the fields are a
  second artifact of kind `handoff_condense` (`application/json`) stored next to the `handoff_view`. The view's bytes
  are not rewritten. Migration 0016 widens `artifact.kind` by the same table rebuild as 0009. `get_artifacts` lists the
  kind (§3).
- **What is sent.** Only non-empty prose, under fixed headings, in this order: `goal`, `constraints`, `instructions`,
  `decisions`, `failed_attempt_notes`, `open_tasks`, `diff_excerpts`. Constraints and instruction text are bullet lines.
  A decision line is `- <kind>: <summary>`. A summary that is only a failure code, an id, a SHA or an integer is
  omitted, and control characters other than newline and tab are stripped.
  Failed-attempt notes are `reported[].text` only (`- summary:` and `- failure_detail:`); `failed_attempts` has no prose
  column, so its codes, commits and artifact ids are not sent. An open-task line is the kind with underscores as words,
  plus string `argv` when the detail is an object; a detail that is a failure code, a pure integer, an id (`run_`,
  `ms_`, `art_`, `sess_`, `dec_`, `evt_`, `apv_`, `gcm_`) or a SHA is dropped, and so are `index`, `outcome`,
  `exit_code` and `run_id`. Diff excerpts are `== <path> ==` plus the text; the `truncated` flag is not sent. Statuses,
  exit codes, counts, run and session ids, commit SHAs and verification output are not sent. Control characters other
  than newline and tab are stripped in the extractor, so a secret split by one is contiguous when the pipeline redacts.
- **Budget.** Sections before `diff_excerpts:` stay whole; the excerpts keep a head of 25% and a tail of 75% of what
  remains (§13.4 step 3). If that header leaves fewer than 200 bytes, head and tail apply to the whole prompt.
- **Validator.** The schema requires exactly the fields that were sent (`additionalProperties: false`, each string
  1..600). Beyond it, all or nothing: one line (no newline, carriage return, tab or other control), no longer than its
  own source field in Unicode code points, and every whole digit run must occur as a whole digit run in the text the
  model was given (redacted, then budgeted), so `17` is not matched by `1700`. There is no URL ban; the table does not
  require one. There is no sanitiser step. Stored text passes the artifact redactor.
- **Fallback and storage.** When the task is enabled and nothing is accepted, every requested field is the raw text cut
  at 600 code points, with `[truncated: <n> characters omitted]` after the cut and not counted in the 600, `source:
  "heuristic"`, no `model_id` and no `template_version`. The same source applies to every field. With the task not
  enabled, nothing is written (§13.7 row 1). An accepted field adds `source: "inner"`, the backend-reported `model_id`
  (passed into `persist`) and `template_version`. Both results record one `handoff_condense` run event whose payload is
  ids, the source, and a map of field name to source: no prose. On accept, the `inner_call` event's `artifact_id` is
  the condensation artifact; on fallback it stays null and the `handoff_condense` event points at the heuristic
  artifact.
- **Output cap.** The raw completion may be at most `4 * (64 + 7 * 600) + 2048` bytes.
- **Trigger.** Nothing calls `condenseHandoffBundle` automatically (the lifecycle may not import the service). The
  `inner test` fixture stays the synthetic stand-in and does not send this task's source content.

*As built (2026-10-14, Delegated, T11.12; table above unchanged), `run_title`:* template `run_title.v1`
(`src/inner/run-title.ts`), a fixed system text that names no product, provider, tool or identifier. Each point is an
**Assumption** unless the table says it:

- **Display-only.** The title and summary are never read by routing, eligibility, policy, retry, approvals or status;
  the module is under `src/inner/` (import-boundary test) and a test scans that nothing outside it imports the task. A
  listing must show the text as data.
- **Input and gate.** The first 1,500 characters of the objective, within `input_budget_bytes`, under the heading
  `Task description:`. The task does not send source content (the objective is not repository content), so only
  `allow_remote` gates a remote backend; this settles the open point at the top of §13.5 as proposed there.
- **Validator.** Beyond the schema (a length over the cap is rejected, not cut): each value is single-line with no
  control character (newline, tab and line or paragraph separators included), no leading or trailing space, no URL and
  no markdown (backtick, `**`, `__`, `~~`, heading, list or quote marker, link, HTML tag). Underscores inside words
  are allowed.
- **Cleaning and sanitiser.** The pipeline strips control characters other than newline and tab before validation. An
  accepted value then has invisible and bidirectional formatting characters removed and whitespace collapsed, passes
  the unchanged §12.4 sanitiser like other drafts (step 10 names only commit and PR text; this task adds it), and is
  capped again by length. A title left empty is `rejected_sanitiser`; a summary left empty is dropped.
- **Record and fallback.** The accepted values ride in the `inner_call` run event payload as `title` and `summary`
  (§13.8); no table or column. `readRunTitle` re-cleans and re-caps what it reads. The fallback is the first non-empty
  line of the objective, cleaned, at most 80 characters by code point, and is returned (with nothing written) when the
  task is not enabled.
- **Trigger.** Nothing calls `createRunTitle` automatically and no listing shows the title yet.

### 13.7 Fallback table

| Situation | `inner_call.outcome` | Result for the caller |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Inner agent not configured, `enabled: false`, task not enabled, or `commit_message` supplied by the agent in `draft_if_missing` | No row | Task fallback; behaviour identical to a build with no inner agent |
| A privacy gate or project setting forbids the call (§13.5) | `fallback_disabled` (reason code) | Task fallback |
| Task suspended after a high rejection rate | `fallback_suspended` | Task fallback until the operator resumes the task |
| Backend unreachable, timeout, error response, open circuit, no free slot, `Ineligible` cli | `fallback_unavailable` (reason code) | Task fallback within the per-call bound; `doctor` shows degraded |
| Output invalid after at most one re-ask | `rejected_validator` | Task fallback |
| Commit or PR text empty or unusable after the sanitiser | `rejected_sanitiser` | Task fallback |
| Output accepted | `accepted` | The validated value |

| Task | Deterministic fallback |
| --- | --- |
| `commit_message` | Tool error `MESSAGE_REQUIRED`; the agent writes the message. Under `always`, the agent's own message when it supplied one |
| `pr_draft` | No artifact |
| `route_features` | Heuristic classifier features, else neutral values |
| `handoff_condense` | Deterministically truncated raw text, labelled |
| `log_summary` | Head and tail excerpt with counts, labelled |
| `run_title` | First line of the objective, truncated |

No fallback depends on a second model call, and no fallback changes a run's state, exit code or verification result.

### 13.8 Telemetry and events

**Span `inner.call`** (added to [§7](#7-telemetry-spans)): one per pipeline run that reaches step 5 or later, parent
`controller.delegate` for a run-linked call, else a root span. Allowed attributes (by construction, no bodies,
ADR-0012): `inner.task`, `inner.backend_kind`, `inner.locality`, `inner.template_version`, `inner.model` (the model ID as
reported or configured), `inner.outcome`, `inner.reason` (short code), `inner.attempts`, `inner.latency_ms`,
`inner.input_bytes`, `inner.output_bytes`, `inner.tokens_in`, `inner.tokens_out` (only when reported),
`inner.breaker_state`, and, for run-linked calls, `run.id` and `session.id`. Forbidden: the prompt, the response, the diff,
log excerpts, objective text, any message text, the backend URL's userinfo. The exporter drops every attribute not on this
list, and a test asserts it.

*As built (2026-10-15, T11.14, Delegated):* the span is **not emitted**. No span exporter exists in the tree, so neither
the span nor the attribute-drop test is built, and the paragraph above stays the design. The pipeline writes one
structured log line named `inner.call` for every pipeline outcome, with or without a ledger (`src/inner/pipeline.ts`),
carrying `task`, `outcome`, `reason`,
`attempts`, `latency_ms`, `input_bytes`, `output_bytes`, `locality`, `breaker_state` and `call_id`, never a body or a
token count (captured logs are asserted free of prompt, response, secret and credential text in
`src/inner/pipeline.test.ts` and `src/inner/http-backend.test.ts`; the line's own contract, exactly that key set and
nothing else, scalar values, one line per outcome including the gate denials that contact no backend, `call_id` equal
to the row id or null without a ledger, and a throwing logger never changing a result, is pinned by
`src/inner/log-line.test.ts`, 2026-10-15 post-#383). The run events in the table below are built as
described (`src/inner/pipeline.test.ts`, `src/inner/suspension.test.ts`). One correction to the last paragraph: the
`doctor` canary writes no `inner_call` row (T11.7 Delegated choice, [ROADMAP §6.6](../ROADMAP.md#66-m11-inner-agent-tasks));
`inner test` writes one with no run.

**Run events** (append-only `run_event.type`, bounded payloads, no text, M11), written only for calls tied to a run:

| Kind | Payload | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `inner_call` | `inner_call_id`, `task`, `outcome`, `reason?`, `locality`, `template_version`, `attempts`, `latency_ms`, `input_bytes`, `output_bytes`; `artifact_id` column set for a `pr_draft` or condensed-text artifact; a `run_title` payload also carries `title` and `summary` (bounded) | Every recorded call |
| `inner_degraded` | `state` (`degraded` or `recovered`), `reason`, `breaker_state`, `backend_kind` | Breaker changes state, or an `Ineligible` probe result changes; written once per transition, not per failed call |
| `inner_task_suspended` | `task`, `window_calls`, `rejected`, `total`, `threshold` | A task is suspended (§13.4 step 11). A resume writes no event; `inner_task_state.resumed_at` records it |

Calls with no run (`inner test`, the `doctor` canary) have no `run_event`; their record is the `inner_call` row, and
`doctor` and `inner status` read `inner_call` and `inner_task_state`. None of these events or outcomes is a run failure
